Russia To Supply Boosters for U.S. Missiles

LD1310070395 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
0657 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By Vitaly Chukseyev]

(FBIS Transcribed Text] WASHINGTON, October 13
(ITAR-TASS) — Ballistic missiles of the former Soviet
Union will compete with American missiles in launch-
ing payloads into space. Following long and difficult
talks in Geneva President Bill Clinton approved a pol-
icy recommended by his aides for science and national
security.

Russians are prepared to revamp and rename as Start
their SS-25 missiles for peaceful uses. Although, the
new U.S. policy covers all foreign-made boosters, for
the start it will apply to Russia and Ukraine.

In addition to the Start booster, Russia is prepared
to supply the refurbished SS-19 renamed Rokot, and
Ukraine, which is seeking access to the American
markets, offers the SS-10.

The first American company to use a Russian booster
is EarthWatch Inc., which intends to launch its first
commercial satellite in the first half of next year.

The Space News weekly, which reported the move does
not conceal that the American booster makers are not
happy about the news. Their representatives conducted
an intense lobbying campaign to prevent competitors
from entry to American markets. The corporations
Orbital Sciences and Lockheed Martin were particularly
concerned.

The U.S. President is expected to issue a special
ordinance in November to provide for the use of foreign
boosters in the United States in line with the existing
bilateral commercial agreements on space problems.

The United States has agreements of this sort with
Russia and China. EarthWatch officials said the launch
of their satellite with the help of the Start booster will
cost, per one pound of payload, just as much as with
the help of the Launch Vehicle offered by Lockheed.

Along with this, the Space News weekly reports that
the U.S. Administration is taking steps to protect the
interests of American manufacturers: Russia has been

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

granted the right to launch not more than eight Western
satellites to the year 2000.

Further on Barents Sea Regional Council Session

MM1310105295 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
13 Oct 95 p3

[Report by Marat Zubko: "Let Us Fly to the Arctic with
Our Northern Neighbors’ Help")

(FBIS Translated Text) Helsinki — During the third ses-
sion of the Barents-European Arctic Regional Council
in the Finnish city of Rovaniemi, the foreign ministers
of Norway, Russia, Finland, Sweden, Iceland, and Den-
mark have advocated the unprecedented development of
a transportation network in Europe’s polar regions:

“To continue the Murmansk (Russia)-Kirkenes (Nor-
way) railroad and to modernize the highway between
the two cities. To build a highway between Petroza-
vodsk and Parikkala in Finland.

“To develop the so-called ‘Barents Corridor,’ envisaging
the construction of a railroad between the Finnish
town of Salla and Alakurtti in Russia, as well as the
“Arkhangelsk Corridor,’ building two railroads.

“To build a new Russian port with oil and gas terminals
at Linakhamari in Pechenga Bay. To modernize the
commercial ports of Murmansk and Arkhangelsk.

“To improve air transportation on the Lulea (Sweden)-
Rovaniemi (Finland)-Murmansk-Arkhangelsk route. To
Carry out work to modernize airports at Petrozavodsk,
Arkhangelsk, and Murmansk...."

Joint programs are also being proposed in the sphere of
power generation, telecommunications, and the modern-
ization of a number of enterprises that pose a threat to
people and the environment. The Barents Council ap-
proved the signing by Norway and Russia of an agree-
ment to modernize the foundries at Nikel and Pechenga.

Needless to say, the question arises of just who will
pay for all this. The Council appealed to national and
international financial institutions. There is hope of
aid from the EU, whose representatives attended the
meeting.

The ministers were particularly concerned about the
problem of recycling the decommissioned nuclear sub-
marines which, as is well known, have built up at naval
bases in northwest Russia.

Next spring, the meeting participants noted, the “clear-
ing up" [sanatsiya] of these Russian submarines should
begin. Touching on this acute problem, Swedish For-
eign Minister Lena Hjelm-Wallen stated:

Anti-Poland Sanction Rumors ‘Greatly
Exaggerated’

MM1210145895 Moscow IZVESTIYA in Russian
12 Oct 95 p3

[Report by Maksim Yusin: "Economic War Against
Poland? Rumors Greatly Exaggerated by One Un-
scrupulous Journalist")

(FBIS Translated Text] Last week Russian-Polish rela-
tions went through some difficult moments. The Krakow
radio station RMF reported to its listeners that Moscow
was preparing to introduce economic sanctions against
Warsaw, citing some very telling statements by Deputy
Foreign Minister Sergey Krylov as confirmation of this.

The author of the material, RMF’s Moscow correspon-
dent, made the following sensational statement, quot-
ing sources in the Foreign Ministry: Displeased with
Poland’s attempts to join NATO, Russia was consider-
ing the possibility of scaling down economic coopera-
tion with Poland, and, in particular, of freezing construc-
tion of a gas pipeline intended to supply Russian gas to
West Europe via Polish territory. The information on
the impending sanctions was followed by a quote from
Sergey Krylov: "Surely the Poles need this gas pipeline?
Surely they need the Russian market?" The suggested
conclusion was clear: The deputy minister was openly
threatening Warsaw.

Persistent attempts by Polish journalists accredited in
Moscow to get through to Krylov on the telephone and
ask him to comment on RMPF’s findings failed. This
merely strengthened the Poles’ suspicions that Russia
really was preparing to declare economic war on their
country.

Approached by IZVESTIYA for an explanation, Sergey
Krylov categorically rejected this assumption: "We
have no intention of introducing any sanctions against
Poland. It is an absurd idea. It is particularly disgraceful
that these notions have been attributed to me. I am
fundamentally opposed to sanctions, because world
practice shows that very rarely is the desired result
achieved with their help."

According to the deputy minister, he has never given
an interview to RMF. He met some Polish television
journalists a few weeks ago. It was then that he

RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS 31

uttered the words used by the Krakow radio station.
However, he assured us that they were spoken in
a totally different context: "We were talking about
Russian-Polish economic cooperation and joint projects
of benefit to both our countries. I cited the contract to
build the gas pipeline as the most striking example; it is
worth a total of $2.4 billion, of which $400 million will
be invested in Polish territory. It was then that I asked
(arguing with those opposed to ties between Poland and
Russia): Surely the Poles need this gas pipeline? Surely
they need the Russian market? Naturally, I made no
reference to any sanctions."

Krylov was extremely vexed that he had been used
"as some kind of bugbear" and set up as virtually
Poland’s number one enemy. In the deputy minister’s
opinion, the journalist who had provoked the scandal
had been extremely unscrupulous. "As I had not said
anything reprehensible, I did not feel it necessary to try
to justify myself and thereby become a laughing stock,"
Krylov said, explaining his reluctance to discuss the ill-
starred interview with Polish journalists. However, we
understood from the deputy minister’s words that he
had not been able to avoid this unpleasant subject in a
conversation with the Polish ambassador.

As far as RMF’s Moscow correspondent is concerned,
this is not the first time he has been at the center of a
scandal. After last year’s incident at Warsaw’s East Sta-
tion (when Polish police beat up some Russian tourists),
the Krakow radio station announced that Boris Yeltsin
had signed an edict to protect his compatriots abroad
which allegedly gave Russian assault forces the right to
cafry out a punitive operation in Polish territory. This
news caused mild panic in Warsaw. Leading politicians
were asked to comment on Moscow’s aggressive sally.
The Poles only calmed down when the text of the edict
was published and it became clear that it deait with
something quite different. Poland, and the incident at
Warsaw’s East Station, were not even mentioned.

Canada

1712 GMT 12 Oct 95

(FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Oct 12 (INTER-
FAX) — The Federal Nuclear and Radiation Safety
Inspectorate, or Gosatomnadzor, plans to sign in mid-
January 1996 a memorandum of understanding and co-
operation with the Atomic Energy Control Board of
Canada.

Yelena Kuznetsova, an official in the Gosatomnadzor
foreign relations board, said that the two agencies will
cooperate in nuclear safety supervision.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

32 RUSSIA INTERNATIONAL AFFAIRS

In particular, she said, they will jointly study safety
standards in nuclear power stations and reactors, in the
production, storage anc transportation of nuclear fuel
and in the production and use of radioactive substances
in the industry, science and medicine.

The Canadians will help their Russian colleagues de-
velop the technology of decommissioning nuclear and
radioatively hazardous installations and draft the legal
framework and the licensing procedure, in particular, of
commissioning power units in nuclear power stations.

Kuznetsova said that the memorandum has been cleared
with the Russian government and the Nuclear Energy
Ministry.

Cooperation in nuclear energy was a major item on
the agenda of Russian Prime Minister Viktor Cher-
nomyrdin’s visit earlier in October. The two sides
agreed on Canadian participation in designing nuclear
power stations for the Russian Far East.

FBIS-SOV-95-198

tion of the major facilities built on Indian territory with

their rich scientific and technical potential.

building, with Indian assistance, of a sea terminal
in Novorossiysk, and on the building with Russian

the rapid progress of our relations," Yarov stressed.

interaction will be to the benefit of both Russia and
India," Yarov declared.

trade and economic, scientific, technical and cultural
cooperation would next meet in the first quarter of
next year to consider implementation of the agreements
reached in New Delhi during the current visit and
prospects for further bilateral cooperation in a variety
of fields.

Yeltsin Rejects ‘Premature Optimism’ Over
Economy

LD1310114995 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1132 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By Andrey Shtorkh)

{[FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW, October 13
(ITAR-TASS) — President Boris Yeltsin today told the
government to step up activity in ensuring stabilisation
of the quality of life of the entire population and every
individual citizen of Russia.

According to presidential press secretary Sergey
Medvedev, the President told participants in today’s
meeting in the Kremlin that during his recent meeting
with Prime Minister Viktor Chernomyrdin he had
criticised the government for the failure to ensure the
timely payment of wages and pensions, as well as
compensations to the investors in banks and firms that
become insolvent.

Boris Yeltsin said he has ordered the Prime Minister to
ensure a turn-around.

According to Sergey Medvedev, the President disagreed
with those who were ready to "sing praises" to the gov-
ernment in connection with "revivification of Russia’s
economic life." The President refused to share "prema-
ture optimism” and said the task was not to “lose pace
but to preserve and consolidate the turn-around” the first
signs of which have appeared in the economic and so-

cial spheres.

The President did not fail to note the positive trends
which have appeared in the country’s social and eco-
nomic situation. He pointed out in particular that one in
every four regions has reported that its industrial pro-
duction has grown since last year. Along with this, the
President subjected to “tough, serious criticism" the sit-
uation in the agro-industrial complex where production
slump was reported this year.

As concerns the draft budget for 1996, the President
called for ruling out "elements of arbitrariness", pointing
out that some of the basic provisions of the draft should
be corrected when finalizing it. He pointed specifically
to the forecast of the country’s social and economic
development and the level of inflation.

the federation, the President noted that the document
was being finalised and it was necessary to take into

parts of the Russian Federation in order to make
necessary changes in time.

Yeltsin noted that the budget potential of the component
parts of the federation had increased considerably but
there were still "elements of arbitrariness" which needed
to be eradicated. The President proposed changing the
principles of interaction between the federal budget and
the budgets of the regions.

The Presidential press secretary explained that "in ac-
cordance with the Constitution of the Russian Federa-
tion, the budgets of the component parts of the Russian

ot RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

Federation are independent, but, taking into considera-
tion a number of economic, geographical and climatic
conditions, relations between the federal budget and the
budgettts of the republics are built proceeding from the
resources which come from that part of the taxes which
are due to the federal budget."

The component parts of the Federation receive financial
assistance from the centre. This year, the process is
“quite tense, but its dynamics is positive," Medvedev
said.

He added that, according to the current plans of the
federal government, next year’s financial assistance
to the subjects of the Federation will become more
substantial. It will be distributed from the federal budget
along four lines, Medvedev said. First of all, a federal
fund of financial support will be formed to compensate
for differences between the spending of the component
parts of the Federation and the revenue due to them
in the regional budgets. Another direction provides for
funding the expenditure for the maintenance of facilities
in the social sphere and the housing fund, which the
republics cannot afford on their own. The third direction
is federal programmes for the development of regions
which have their specifics in each republic. Finally, the
fourth direction of assistance from the federal budget to
the budgets of the republics provides for subsidies to
individual administrative and territorial entities in case
they experience temporary financial difficulties.
According to Medvedev, "the existing practice of inter-
action between the federal and regional budgets does not
meet new requirements." In this context, the meeting in
the Kremlin discussed ways of improving the existing
system. In particular, the Russian government proposed
introducing "for the next three years, stable rates and
norms of assignations from the federal budget to the
budgets of the component parts of the Russian Federa-
tion,” Medvedev said.

He recalled that this year the Federal fund of financial
support of the component parts of the Federation was
formed on the basis of 27-percent deductions from
the value-added tax collected throughout the Russian
Federation. Next year, resources channelled to the fund
would be made up of 15-percent deductions from the
amount of the federal budget tax revenues.

According to government experts, this would ensure
a steady inflow of resources to the budgets of the
republics.

. The need for specific changes to the draft federal budget
for 1996 was stressed by the President in the course
of today’s meeting in the Kremlin. Yeltsin said that
the forecast for the country’s social and economic

1996 budget. Both First Vice-Premier Anatoliy Chubays
and Minister for Nationalities and Regional Policy

Yeltsin Signs Edict on Constitutional Conformity

MMI1210142495 Moscow ROSSIYSKAYA GAZETA
in Russian 11 Oct 95 p 3

{"Edict No. 1007 of the Russian Federation President
on Measures To Ensure Collaboration Between the
Federal Organs of State Power and the Organs of State
Power of the Russian Federation Components During
the Implementation of Constitutional and Legal Reform
in the Russian Federation Components," signed by
Russian Federation President B. Yeltsin in the Kremlin,
Moscow, on 5 October — from the “Documents”
section]

(FBIS Translated Text] With the aim of ensuring that
the Constitutions and laws of the republics and the
charters, laws, and other statutory legal acts of the krays,
oblasts, cities with federal status, the autonomous oblast,
and the autonomous okrugs conform to the Russian
Federation Constitution and to federal laws; ensuring
collaboration between the federal organs of state power
and the organs of state power of the Russian Federation
components during the implementation of constitutional
and legal reform in the Russian Federation components;
and eliminating disagreements which may arise between
the legislative (representative) and executive organs
of state power of the Russian Federation components
during the adoption of statutory legal acts; and guided
by Article 80 of the Russian Federation Constitution, I
decree:

1. That violation of the Russian Federation Constitution
and federal laws by the organs of state power of the
Russian Federation components during the elaboration
and adoption of Constitutions and laws by the republics
and of charters, laws, and other statutory legal acts
by the krays, oblasts, cities of federal status, the
autonomous oblast, and autonomous okrugs, is deemed
impermissible.

2. The organs of state power of the Russian Federation
components are instructed:

is report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

— to remove any contradictions with the provisions
of the Russian Federation Constitution and the federal
laws which exist in the Constitutions and laws of the
republics and the charters, laws, and other statutory legal
acts of the krays, oblasts, cities with federal status, the
autonomous oblast, and the autonomous okrugs;

— in drafting and adopting statutory legal acts of
Russian Federation components, to adhere strictly to
the provisions of the Russian Federation Constitution,
which enshrine as the basis of the Russian Federation’s
constitutional system a federal structure for the Russian
Federation based on its state integrity, the unity of the
system of state power, the delimitation of spheres of
jurisdiction and powers between the federal organs of
state power and the organs of state power of the Russian
Federation components, and on the equality and self-
determination of the peoples in the Russian Federation;

— on the basis of the provisions of Articles 73, 76, and
77 of the Russian Federation Constitution, pending the
publication of the federal law on the general principles
of the organization of the representative and executive
organs of state power of the Russian Federation compo-
nents, the Russian Federation components are to refrain
from adopting statutory legal acts which establish or-
ganizational principles or a system of organs of state
power in the Russian Federation components.

3. A Commission Under the Russian Federation Presi-
dent for Collaboration Between the Federal Organs Of
State Power and the Organs of State Power of the Rus-
sian Federation Components During the Implementa-
tion of Constitutional and Legal Reform in the Russian
Federation Components (hereinafter called the Commis-
sion) is to be formed.

The Commission is responsible for:

— ensuring collaboration between the federal organs
of state power and the organs of state power of the
Russian Federation components during the process of
the elaboration and adoption of the Constitutions and
laws of the republics and the charters, laws, and other
Statutory legal acts of krays, oblasts, cities with federal
Status, the autonomous oblast, and the autonomous
okrugs and during the resoluiion of other questions of
constitutional and legal reform in the Russian Federation
components;

— elaborating measures to ensure that the Constitutions
and laws of republics and the charters, laws, and
other normative legal acts of the krays, obiasts, cities
with federal status, the autonomous oblast, and the
autonomous okrugs conform to the Russian Federation
Constitution and federal laws;

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 35

— eliminating possible disagreements between the fed-
eral organs of state power and the organs of state power
of the Russian Federation components and also between
the legislative (representative) and executive organs of
State power of the Russian Federation components;

— drafting proposals on the application by the Rus-
sian Federation president of conciliation procedures to
resolve such differences in line with Article 85 of the
Russian Federation Constitution.

4. The Russian Federation President’s State Legal Ad-
ministration is responsible for providing legal support
for the Commission’s activity, forming a special sub-
department within its structure and increasing its per-
sonnel by 25; and the Russian Federation Presidential
Staff Administration for Working With Territories and
other structural subdepartments of the Russian Feder-
ation Presidential Staff, as well as the Office of the
Administrator of Presidential Affairs are responsible for
providing organizational and material and technical sup-
port for the Commission’s activity.

5. The leader of the Russian Federation Presidential
Staff, with the participation of the interested federal
organs of state power and organs of state power of
the Russian Federation components, is to present for
ratification by the Russian Federation president within
two months a statute on the Commission Under the
Russian Federation President for Collaboration Between
the Federal Organs Of State Power and the Organs
of State Power of the Russian Federation Components
During the Implementation of Constitutional and Legal
Reform in the Russian Federation Components, and a
proposal for its composition.

6. The Russian Federation Government is to adopt the
necessary measures to strengthen collaboration with the
organs of executive power of the Russian Federation
components in legislative activity.

7. The present edict comes into force from the moment
of its publication.

[Signed] Russian Federation President B. Yeltsin
[Dated] Moscow, the Kremlin, 5 October 1995

Daily Reports Council on Cadre Policy 11 Oct
Session

MM1210103995 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 12 Oct 95 pl

[Report by Pavel Anokhin and Vladislav Krukovskiy:
"Deeds, Not Length of Service, Determine an Official’s
Standing")

(FBIS Translated Text] Yesterday’s [11 October] ses-
sion of the Council on Cadre Policy under the Rus-
sian Federation president considered the draft presiden-
tial edicts "On Qualification Requirements for Holding
State Office in the Federal State Service" and “On Ex-
penditure Norms for Federal State Officials on Official
Travel Within the Russian Federation" and discussed the
national report on the question of "State Administration
and Development" for the UN General Assembly’s 50th
session.

Recently the Kremlin has clearly stepped up its efforts
on questions of developing the system of state admin-
istration. The head of state recently signed the edict
“On Urgent Measures To Improve Work With Cadres
Within the Russian Federation State Service System,"
and fresh documents are already being prepared in its
further development. Thus, the draft edict "On Qual-
ification Requirements..." suggests tightening up these
requirements as regards the length of service and work
experience of federal state officials, defines the prac-
tice for their promotion to suitable office, and envisages
" measures to prevent unjustified cadre transfers.

For example, in order to hold one of the top state offices,
a candidate must have at least 15 years of state service.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

The problem of state officials’ length of service
prompted the lengthiest discussion among Council
members. It was emphasized that it is not always right
to absolutize this indicator when appraising various
officials. During the discussion of this problem it was
proposed to amplify the actual formulation of the pres-
idential edict. The following wording was agreed: "On
Uniform Qualification Requirements for the Length of
Service and Remuneration of State Officials."

Another draft of the edict "On Expenditure Norms for
Federal State Officials on Official Travel Within the
Russian Federation" lays down a uniform procedure in
this important work. The draft envisages differentiated
norms for such expenditures depending on the office
held and the price index. Council members proposed
that, in individual cases, the document should provide
for supernormative expenditure of official travel funds.

While discussing the draft presidential edicts, the Coun-
cil members expressed alarm in connection with the de-
lay in submitting to the State Duma the draft law "On
State Service." It was decided to convene a special con-
ference on this question, to be hosted by Sergey Filatov,
chief of the Russian Federation Presidential Staff. The
Council on Cadre Policy under the president approved
its work plan for October-December 1995.

Council Discusses Local Self-Government

MM1210100495 Moscow ROSSIYSKIYE VESTI
in Russian 12 Oct 95 pl

[Report by Lyubov Tsukanova: "Self-Government
Without Pressure. Initiative From Below Enjoys Sup-
port from President and Government")

(FBIS Translated Text] On opening the first session of
the Council on Local Self-Government, a consultative
organ under the Russian Federation president, Prime
Minister Viktor Chernomyrdin announced that his cab-
inet would examine in the very near future a federal
program in support of local self-government.

This program is one of the first practical documents
aimed at implementing the Law on Local Self-
Government. Everyone knows the difficulties and
acrimonious contradictions which accompanied this
law’s elaboration and adoption. Now it is accepted for
implementation with just as many difficulties, since its
essence is to redistribute power.

The lengthy period of time since the adoption of the -

Russian Federation Constitution, Minister for National-
ities Affairs Vyacheslav Mikhaylov said, has led to nu-
merous complications at regional level. While there was
no law, local self-government was taking shape haphaz-
ardly, accompanied by the development of progressive
and dead-end tendencies alike. But even now, when the
Law on General Principles of Local Self-Government
has come into force, its provisions are clearly inade-
quate for establishing a smooth system of power at the
local level. It is necessary, as Viktor Chernomyrdin said,
to adopt more than 20 federal laws developing and am-
plifying the “general principles."

The main knot of problems is to be found in the sphere
of mutual relations between regional organs of power
and organs of local self-government. City mayors unan-
imously assert that the authorities in Russian Federation
components have given a “poor” welcome to the Law on
Local Self-Government since they are unwilling to let
cities (which are now objects of self-government) slip
away from their subordination. The governors, for their
part, fear the loss of governability since, as Moscow
Oblast Administration Head Anatoliy Tyazhlov said,
“the executive power is answerable to the population for
everything.” The problem, as many people have noted,
lies in the fact that there is no clear-cut and unambigu-
ous definition of territorial formations which are objects
of local self-government. This question must be resolved
either by a special federal law or by Federation compo-
nent laws.

It is generally believed that local self-government
will be unable to properly stand on its own two feet

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 37

until such time as solutions are provided to ques-
tions like budget and financial regulation, formation
of organizational-legal foundations of local self-
government, and provision of cadres and information
technology for it. Local self-government heads insist on
long-term (for at least three or four years) guarantees
of the main sources of budget revenues for municipal
formations and on solution to the problem of trans-
ferring land and real estate to municipal ownership,
without which it would be impossible to ensure at
least some stable and forecastable development. But
even now, while many of the most complex questions
still remain unsolved, the law offers an opportunity
to develop local self-government, this "foundation of
the state’s stability," as Nikolay Travkin defined it.
He does not share the “universal lament" about the
allegedly catastrophic state of affairs in the regions:
Things are still getting done and much depends on
people. Viktor Dorkin, mayor of Moscow Oblast’s city
of Dzerzhinskiy, agrees with him and also believes that
much depends on the initiative of people who head the
organs of local self-government.

Nikolay Travkin correctly noted that the question of ac-
countability is a question of elections when the popu-
lation itself will be able to decide who is capable of
heading the process of restructuring life in its com-
munity. The elections are yet another tense aspect of
the process of establishing the local self-government
system. The legislators, having prescribed that elec-
tions to local self-government organs should be held
in six months’ time, have, in the opinion of many prac-
titioners, “rushed ahead" somewhat. Many Federation
components simply lack the legislative basis for this
(which, incidentally, prompted the Russian Federation
president’s edict proposing that local elections be post-
poned until December 1996). But many practitioners do
not advise any artificial delays to the electoral process
in places which are comprehensively prepared for it.
The main point, and members of the Council on Local
Self-Government are convinced of this, is that it is nec-
essary to avoid the process of politicizing the election
campaign and to prevent any speculation on this topic.

Local self-government is a vast field for activity and, to
all intents and purposes, it “has neither been plowed
nor sown" in our country. But, as Sergey Filatov
remarked, "things are not altogether bad," there are some
“encouraging signs" which ought to be nurtured. The
establishment of effective local authority will take n.any
years, there is no need for haste so as not to inflict any
damage in the “rush,” but there is no need either to
postpone this work “until later" and wait for the creation
of all conditions. Local self-government, Sergey Filatov

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

38 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

said, must be created through joint efforts, it is a
guarantee of Russia’s transformation.

Panels Examine [legal Migration, Export Problems
MM1310133295 Moscow KRASNAYA ZVEZDA
in Russian 13 Oct 95 pl

(Russian Federation Security Council Press Service
report: ““Migration Threat’ to Our Interests")

(FBIS Translated Text] Moscow — On 11 October
1995 there was a joint session of two interdepartmental
commissions of the Russian Federation Security Council
— the commission on border policy, chaired by Andrey
Nikolayev, director of the Federal Border Service, and
the commission on public security and the fight against
crime and corruption, chaired by Valentin Kovalev,
Russian Federation justice minister.

The session examined the question "On Measures To
Prevent Illegal Migration on the Territory of the Russian
Federation.” It was noted that Russia has become a mag-
net for migrants from almost all parts of the world. Ille-
gal migration, especially from states in Asia and Africa,
China, and the Near and Middle East, poses a threat to
national interests. The question "On the Activity of the
Russian Federation Customs Organs in Preventing the
legal Export of Strategically Important Raw Materials"
was also examined. It was noted that there have been
instances when foreign economic activity has been ac-
tively used to launder funds obtained by criminal means.

Military Issues

General Smirnov Discusses Military Draft Problems

MMI1110091295 Moscow KRASNAYA ZVEZDA
in Russian 7 Oct 95 pl

{Interview with Lieutenant General Vasiliy Smirnov,
deputy chief of the Russian Federation Armed Forces
General Staff Main Organizational and Mobilization
Directorate, by Oleg Falichev; place and date not given:
"Fall Draft Should Proceed in Strict Accordance with
the Federal Law")

{(FBIS Translated Excerpt] [Falichev] Vasiliy Vasilye-
vich, the attitude of society and the state toward the
army is formed from many criteria. There is supply and
financing.... But perhaps one of the most important is
its staffing. For some years now this process has been
proceeding with great difficulties, to put it mildly. The
reasons for this phenomenon are a subject for a separate
talk. But today let us talk about results. And here we
should obviously begin with the spring draft campaign.
After all, you will agree that the press has said virtually
nothing about its final results.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

[Smirnov] Perhaps.... Especially as the problems of the
1995 spring and fall draft remain among the most acute.
Unfortunately some of our citizens have formed the
persistent opinion that the new draftees are needed for
the army, for the military department, and not for the
country. Although the Constitution speaks directly of
citizens’ duty to defend their Fatherland. As before
many people are saying that the military commissariat is
“seizing” draftees for the army. Yet in accordance with
existing legislation the organization and execution of the
military draft are assigned to the local administration
heads. It is largely precisely thanks to their attention
toward this state task that the target for the drafting
of citizens set by the Russian Federation president was
fulfilled in the spring.

Social structures also played a positive role in carrying
out the draft. Structures like the Russian defense techni-
cal sports organization, the “Council of parents of Rus-
sian servicemen" movement, and a number of others.
At the same time someone was, figuratively speaking,
putting a spoke in the wheels. Unfortunately we have
to note the adverse influence exerted on the draft by the
"Soldiers’ Mothers of Russia" movement and the "com-
mittee of soldiers’ mothers of Russia." They organized
picketing of the draft and assembly points, issued ille-
gal ultimatums to the organs of state power, and incited
citizens not to fulfill the requirements of the relevant
laws.

Alas, we have instances of the nonfulfillment of the
law’s requirements by the organs of local power. Thus
the heads of the local administrations of Naberezhnyye
Chelny and Aznakayevskiy Rayon in the Republic of
Tatarstan dodged leadership in carrying out the military
draft. As a result the draft was in fact disrupted in these
regions.

[Falichev] That is the laws were broken by people
whose official duty was to keep track of their fulfill-
ment?

{Smirnov} Yes, and that is very regrettable. Moreover,
some officials even impeded the draft. I have in mind
the leaders of individual enterprises, organizations, and
institutions. Thus the rector of the Orenburg regional
institute of management tried to disrupt the notification
of the institute’s graduates and their appearance at the
draft commissions. And the prosecutor’s office organs,
despite appeals from the military commissariats, took
no effective steps against the violator of legislation.
Well! After that can we be surprised that the number
of “dodgers” today is already in excess of 27,000.

[Falichev] As we know in the spring amendments were
adopted to the law “on military service obligation and
military service." But to this day passions around them

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

have not abated in parliament. The Duma defense com-
mittee adopted its own “amendments to the amend-
ments” in the summer.... What kind of a situation do we
have here? What has happened recently on this "front?"

[Smirnov] Indeed, on 29 April the law “on the intro-
duction of changes and additions to the Russian Fed-
eration law ‘on military service obligation and military
service’” was adopted. In accordance with this law the
duration of military service upon drafting is set at 24
months. Which will make it possible as early as this
fall to staff the troops to 85-90 percent. But there was
no complete clarity here until the very last moment.
Why? You rightly mentioned the “amendments to the
amendments.” On 29 July this year they were suggested
by the State Duma. If they had gotten through then this
would have had the most adverse effect on the staffing
level of the troops and would have virtually canceled
out all the preceding lawmaking work. Judge for your-
self. The troops’ staffing level would have declined to
50 percent (!) and each soldier and sergeant would have
had to perform the duties of two or even three service-
men. Combat capable formations and units would have
disappeared in the Armed Forces.

We must pay tribute to the Federation Council. It
approached the examination of these “amendments” in a
quite serious and balanced manner. As a result they were
rejected at the session 4 October this year. So that the
draft will proceed in accordance with the law adopted
in the spring. [passage omitted]

{Falichev] And one last question, Vasiliy Vasilyevich.
How long will the soldiers and sergeants recruited
earlier serve? In particular is release into the reserve
being delayed for those who were drafted for 18 months
in 1994 under the old law?

[Smirnov] I must stress once again that the changes
introduced to the law this spring will now operate. They
affect those who were drafted earlier, for 18 months, for
instance. I shall explain. The 29 April 1995 federal law
“on the introduction of changes and additions to the
Russian Federation law ‘on military service obligation
and military service’" for servicemen who are serving
under the draft now sets the term of this service at 24
months. And for those with higher education the term is
12 months. Article 4 of this law introduces these terms
of military service as of 1 October this year and extends
them to servicemen drafted previously and undergoing
military service under the draft.

Thus, this fall only those servicemen who were drafted
to military service in the fall of 1993 will be released
into the reserve. As will those who have higher educa-
tion and were drafted in the fall of 1994. Young men

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 39

drafted to military service in April-June 1994 will be
released into the reserve in the spring of 1996.

Says Defense Spending To Be Increased

LD1310110595 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1024 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By Gennadiy Yezhov]

(FBIS Transcribed Text) MOSCOW, October 13
(ITAR-TASS) — Russian Prime Minister Viktor
Chernomyrdin said Friday that his government was
ready to reconsider and increase the country’s defence
appropriations as compared to the figures of the 1996
draft budget.

The budget envisages 78.9 trillion roubles (over 17
billion USD) for the purpose, while, according to
sources in the Defence ministry, the military demand
111 trillion roubles (25 billion USD) saying the current
budget figures will not allow the armed forces to be a
reliable guarantor of national security and state integrity
of Russia.

Chernomyrdin did not disclose how much the govern-
ment may add, but told the State Duma that the "govern-
ment was ready to earmark more means for the upkeep
of the army, for buying fuel, food, and the necessary
supplies”.

The premier also promised that the construction of
housing for servicemen would be continued and money
for this would be obtained, among other sources, from
the existing stock markets.

Chernomyrdin said the government deemed it necessary
to continue the development of new military equipment
in order to “possess powerful and highly effective armed
forces". It also intends to expand the export of Russian
armaments.

In spite of the existing difficulties in the national
economy, Russian scientists were able to develop in
1995 several new models of air force equipment, new
types of "air-to-air" missiles, new anti-aircraft missile
complexes, as well as some efficient means for the
Strategic nuclear forces. The Russian army received
a new combat helicopter, developed by the Kamov
Designing Bureau, and several new warships, according
to Chernomyrdin.

ments", said Viktor Chernomyrdin, chairman of the Rus-

being done based on proposals by the Association of
Russian Banks, he noted.

(ITAR-TASS) — Prime Minister Viktor Chernomyrdin

enterprises were actively looking for a niche of their

can be found in any sector, although there are still
enterprises which he described as "lying down."

resolution on material liability of the debtor enterprises.

to other enterprises or to their own employees would be
liable for their debts with their property.

Duma Compromise Vote on Federation Council
Reported

MK1310100995 Moscow KOMMERSANT-DAILY
in Russian 12 Oct 95 pp 1, 3

[Report by Yevgeniy Yurev and Veronika Kutsyllo:
"State Duma Plenary Session. Federation Council’s
Future Will Be Decided by President All the Same")

(FBIS Translated Text] Yesterday the State Duma
rushed, through three votes at once, the [draft] law
"On Formation of the Federation Council." It differs
substantially from the president’s draft: the latter pro-
posed that members of the Federation Council could
be representatives of executive and legislative bodies
of power in the regions, whereas the deputies have re-
placed representatives with [administration] heads. The
heads of [local] administrations in the Federation Coun-
cil should necessarily be elected by the population, and
those appointed by the president should face elections
within eight months. The [draft] law is most likely to
be turned down by Yeltsin in the same manner the pre-
ceding [draft] law was.

The situation surrounding the formation of the upper
chamber looked to be going nowhere after the president
rejected the [draft] law passed by the State Duma and
the Federation Council on the election of [Federation
Council] deputies. Attempts made by a conciliation
commission to reach a compromise between having the
deputies elected and the principle of appointing [them]
proposed by the president proved futile. The uncertainty
of the Federation Council’s further fate a little more than
two months before the powers of [the current Federation
Council] deputies expire and the prospect of having no
legitimate upper chamber at all pressured the [Duma]
deputies into accepting the draft law proposed by the
president.

42 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

The concession the Duma had to make is, in fact, condi-
tional. Amendments to the presidential variant suggested
by Vladimir Isakov, Duma Legislation Committee chair-
man, considerably alter its meaning. This is in fact anew
version of the (draft) law. If one reads carefully Isakov’s
amendments, which became law in no time, many curi-
ous things can be discovered. At first glance, the Duma
agreed with the president by deciding against the purely
elective principle. Compared with the president’s vari-
ant, the Duma made the law even tougher: Members
of the upper chamber cannot b« merely two represen-
tatives of a region’s executive and legislative author-
ity, but specifically the heads of a region’s authorities.
However, the provision that only heads of administra-
tions who were elected by the population can be Fed-
eration Council members clashes with Yeltsin’s famous
edict on "spacing" elections, according to which elec-
tions for governor were scheduled for December 1996.
Under the Duma law, elections for heads of administra-
tion are supposed to be held within eight months of the
law’s entry into force; that is to say, if the Federation
Council approves the [draft] law by the end of October
and the president signs it into law, the deadline for such
elections would virtually coincide with the presidential
elections in June 1996.

Furthermore, under the [draft] law passed yesterday, the
Federation Council might never go to work (at least it
will surely not go to work in December): The Federation
Council is regarded as competent if its members number
at least three quarters of the total number (three quarters
of 178 is 134). At the present time, there are in Russia
87 heads of legislative power (Chechnya and Ulyanovsk
Oblast have none); there are 10 or so elected heads
of administration (experts have doubts about Yuriy
Luzhkov, who was elected by the population as deputy
mayor, while it was Yeltsin who appointed him mayor);
and there are some 20 popularly elected presidents of
republics that are part of Russia.

The formation of [the next] Federation Council is being
made even more of a problem by modest notes to the
effect that its members can be heaus of legislative and
executive bodies of power “elected in correspondence
with the law." It is logical to assume that reference is
made to some laws defining a procedure for electing
bodies of local power. But chairmen of local legislative
assemblies in most oblasts were elected according to an
established procedure rather than according to a law —
that is to say, such laws need to be thought up, and then
elections can be held according to them.

It is not hard to predict the future of the [draft] law
— voting against it will be advocates of the original
election variant (90 deputies voted for it on 28 July),
and simply sober politicians who realize that such a law

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

would not make it possible to form a chamber that will
be able to work. The [draft] could become law if the
president agrees to elect all governors on 17 December,
but this is extremely improbable.

Equally improbable is that in thinking up his amend-
ments, Vladimir Isakov did not realize that the law
would not work. This must have been the only objec-
tive — to provoke the president into displaying a lack
of flexibility and into turning down the [draft] law. The
plan, if there was such, plays right into Boris Yeltsin’s
hands: According to KOMMERSANT-DAILY’s infor-
mation, no special hopes were pinned on the Duma,
and sending the president’s version to the deputies was
merely a goodwill gesture — the presidential edict on
the formation of the Federation Council has long been
awaiting its hour (in particular, definition by the Con-
Stitutional Court of the term "formation"). Incidentally,
it is possible that a seat for Vladimir Shumeyko may be
found in it — neither the president’s nor Isakov’s texts
provide for any Russian Federation representatives at
all.

Setting rather high standards for future deputies, the
current ones proved far too lenient toward themselves
and disagreed with a proposal on amending the law on
deputies’ status that too broadly interprets the deputies’
immunity: An attempt to narrow the limits of deputies’
immunity was rejected.

Duma Rejects Draft Law Limiting Deputies’
Immunity

MK1310114395 Moscow SEGODNYA in Russian
12 Oct 95 pl

[Report by Petr Zhuraviev: “The Majority of Duma
Deputies Are Not Yet Ready To Give Up Some of Their
Privileges"}

{[FBIS Translated Text] Yesterday, the State Duma
failed to pass amendments to the law on the status
of a deputy, envisioning a reduction in deputies’ level
of immunity. Only 180 parliament members voted to
approve the amendments on the first reading.

In accordance with the current law on status, a criminal
case against a member of the State Duma or the
Federation Council can only be initiated (or an arrest
or search can only be made) after the consent of
the chamber is obtained. Yesterday, Deputy Grigoriy
Bondarev proposed that the law be amended so that
the chambers would interfere at a later stage and give
consent not on the initiation of a case, but with its
transfer to a court. "A deputy’s office has become
very attractive for criminal elements," Mr. Bondarev
emphasized in his speech at the session. In his words, if

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

the amendments are passed, law-enforcement agencies
will only be able to interrogate a deputy; as regards his
search or arrest, everything will remain as it is now.

Mr. Bondarev was criticized by nearly everyone who
took part in the debate — some for the "rigidity,"
others for the “softness” of his approach. Among the
former was Vladimir Zhirinovskiy, who accused the
proponent of distorting “the very essence of immunity.”
Viktor Ilyukhin, head of the Security Committee, in turn
emphasized that Mr. Bondarev proposed "a somewhat
limited version” of amendments to the law. In Mr.
Ilyukhin’s opinion, immunity should not be extended
to deputies who “have committed crimes not related to
their official activity.”

As a result, Mr. Ilyukhin’s committee was commis-
sioned yesterday to elaborate, together with Mr. Bon-
darev, draft amendments and submit them to one of the
next sessions.

Duma Urges Government To End State Pensions
Crisis

LD1310110895 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1034 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By Ivan Novikov]

{FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW October 13 (ITAR-
TASS) — The State Duma has suggested that the gov-
ernment work out within two weeks a comprehensive
plan of measures to take the state pensions system out
of the crisis.

At a plenary meeting here on Friday, the Duma adopted
a Bill “On measures to ensure the timely payment
of pensions in the fourth quarter of 1995". The Bill
determines a procedure for urgent financial aid to the
Pensions Foundation in the fourth quarter in view of the
critical situation concerning the payment of pensions.

With a view to radically changing the situation, the
Central Bank has been advised to grant a non-interest
credit of 3,000,000 million roubles to the Pensions
Foundation in October this year for a term of six months
resultant of selling part of currency or gold reserve.

It is now up to the Federation Council to confirm or
turn down the Duma-adopted Bill.

Yeltsin Pledges ‘No More War’

LD1310090795 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
0843 GMT 13 Oct 95

[By Andrey Shtorkh]

(FBIS Transcribed Text} MOSCOW October 13 (ITAR-
TASS) — Russian President Boris Yeltsin pledged on
Friday that there will be no more war in Chechnya
despite all provocations by militants loyal to separatist
leader Dzhokhar Dudayev.

“We shall not sidetrack from the peaceful path no matter
how Dzhokhar Dudayev pushes us to that", the president
told a meeting of regional governors.

“The political stability in the whole of Russia depends
on what the situation will be in the Chechen republic",
the president stressed.

Talks about a possible state of emergency in Chech-
nya began after assassination attempts were staged in
Groznyy on presidential envoy Oleg Lobov and federal
troops commander Anatoliy Romanov. Lobov escaped
unharmed, while Romanov is still in coma in a Moscow
hospital.

Russian top military officials backed the introduction of
the emergency situation which could have triggered the
resumption of combat activities, but Yeltsin refused.

"We shall help Chechnya materially, in the sphere of
construction, economic restoration, in paying pensions
which the population have not received under the
Dudayev regime for three years", Yeltsin said.

“We shall also pay wages and fight unemployment in
the Chechen republic", the president said adding that
Dudayev had brought Chechnya to a complete collapse.

Volskiy Thinks War Not To Be Resumed
MK1310061195 Moscow OBSHCHAYA GAZETA
in Russian
No. 41, 12-18 Oct 95 (Signed to press 11 Oct 95) p 1
{Interview with Arkadiy Volskiy by Vitaliy Yaro-
shevskiy; place and date not given: “The Time of Sab-
otage")
(FBIS Translated Text] [Yaroshevskiy] Two months ago
you promised to name the representatives of the “party
of war" in the Kremlin and have not done so to date.
Can you do it now?

: may contain Shsed material Coovina and di ee
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

As RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

[Volskiy] The question is not about personalities. There
is a certain group, mainly the military, who believe
that first the Dudayevites had to be finished off and
then negotiations conducted. With whom? And where?
At the cemetery? And what does finishing off mean?
The local authorities say that a mere 1,000 Dudayevites
are left. Some even say 600. Even if they are 10
times as numerous, they can still be finished off. But
Dudayevism today has affected the entire republic.
Chechens sympathizing with Dudayev have spread
around in cities and villages. In daytime they plow the
land, and at night they take up arms. Who is there
to finish off? The Chechen people? Even according
to official figures, 26,000 civilians have already been
killed. And the local government cites figures three
times as high.

[Yaroshevskiy] After the attack on Lobov and Romanov
there is now talk about a mysterious "third force." What
is it?

[Volskiy] I would say this: There should be a will to
look for this force. The impression is that no one wants
to find the organizers of the attack. This worries me
more than anyihing else.

Here is one terrible example. In late July the Chapanov
family was murdered in a Groznyy suburb: four kids, a
woman, and an old man. This shocked all of Groznyy;
the negotiations were on the verge of collapse. Criminal
proceedings were instituted. The Russian president
stated that he would personally monitor the progress
of the investigation. My understanding is that once the
president has made such a statement, it is necessary
to set up a special group at the top level. In fact the
investigation of this monstrous crime was entrusted to
the local prosecutor’s office, which has no access to
either the Dudayevites or the troops.

The same happened with the attack on Lobov. The
Dudayevites swear that they stand to lose from this
assassination attempt. Maskhadov confirmed this, as
a military man: The leaves are falling; the forest is
becoming bare. The gunmen are clearly visible. Should
hostilities resume, they will simply be wiped out in their
mountain camps.

After the attack on Romanov, Maskhadov said that he
would personally execute those who had blown up his
friend. But nothing has changed. Therefore you can talk
seriously about a third force only when the investigation
is Carried on reasonably. But it has to be conducted in
the first place. There are clear-cut traces; the criminals
laid 250 meters of wire from the explosives to the
detonator. Shortly before the attack on Romanov, the
car of Mikhaylov, head of the Russian delegation at
the negotiations in Groznyy, came under fire — exactly

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

from the bridge on Minutka Square, under which the
general’s car was blown up. There was nothing about
this in the press, although the car had come under
serious attack. Thank God everyone lived. Mikhaylov
did not report the attack. After that armored personnel
Carriers and patrols were stationed on the bridge. Why
were they withdrawn several days before the terrorist
act against Romanov?

And the main thing: The pattern in Lobov’s and
Romanov’s cases is the same. Even the wires and
detonators are the same. What else is there to say?
Therefore I believe that no one is really looking for
the criminals.

[Yaroshevskiy] Romanov should have been given extra
protection. What has happened is yet another failure by
our special services?

[Volskiy] There is some prehistory to this. Some 40
minutes before the attack, Romanov had arranged to
meet with Khasbulatov. In all likelihood, somebody who
was very well informed and stood very near the general
in both a direct and a figurative way prompted him
toward this meeting in order to expedite the terrorist
act. Romanov could not have bypassed Minutka. This
was known. Someone did everything very expeditiously.
The charges were suspended under the bridge and it was
easy to install them quickly, especially if there was no
security.

The third force is rather powerful. Look at the
Khadzhiyev government. From the first day they
have said: We must control Russian troops. Romanov
categorically rejected that. Gantemirov, the mayor of
Groznyy, invariably objected to the participation of
OSCE officials in the negotiations. I saw him send
several warnings to the OSCE headquarters: You must
leave Groznyy. Romanov for his part supported the
European diplomats. He followed a reasonable policy
and as a result made enemies, even among those who
are sympathetic to Russia. Therefore the investigation
should be comprehensive.

[Yaroshevskiy] Is there a limit to the Russians’ pa-
tience? What about the Chechens?

{Vulskiy] The patience of the Russian people has run
out. I accidentally got aboard an aircraft carrying 16
coffins with dead soldiers to Samara. I know what
sorrow means; I saw it with my own eyes. But if a
large-scale war begins, there will be no fewer casualties.
Chechen society is divided, but the majority still want
the war to end.

[Yaroshevskiy] The Russian “delegates” in Groznyy
are not free people. There are guidelines for them to
follow from above; there is an action plan. But still:

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

Have Moscow instructions always been in line with
your convictions? Has it ever happened that “ultimate
considerations" ran against your conscience?

[Volskiy] Probably not. I am not a flatterer. But I must
say that both the president and the prime minister are
clearly set on calming society down before the elections.

[Yaroshevskiy] The military agreements have been
put on hold. Minister Kulikov insists that a state of
emergency be imposed. President Yeltsin is thinking
about it....

[Volskiy] It was last December, when we were bringing
troops into Chechnya, that a state of emergency should
have been imposed. Now is not the best time. If a state
of emergency is imposed, the interior troops will not
be enough. Therefore, a state of emergency should only
be imposed in Groznyy, at most, in this one city, while
nearly half of the Chechen villages — approximately
400 — live a quiet life. These are zones free from the
gunmen. Should a state of emergency also be imposed
there? Russian presidential advisers divided 3:2. Three
are against the state of emergency and two are in favor.

[Yaroshevskiy] What is awaiting us after all? Will a
new war begin?

[Volskiy] I don’t think so, although a day before the
attack on Romanov [Defense Minister] Grachev actu-
ally advocated the resumption of war. A coincidence?
Possibly. Nonetheless, you should carefully watch the
moves of the power structures.

More on Shkirko as Troop Commander

LD1210210695 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1924 GMT 12 Oct 95

(FBIS Transcribed Text] MOSCOW, Oct 12 (INTER-
FAX) — By order of the Russian interior minister,
Lieutenant-General Anatoliy Shkirko on Thursday was
appointed the commander of the combined contingent
of federal troops in Chechnya.

Shkirko has replaced General Anatoliy Romanov, who
is in a serious condition at the Burdenko military
hospital in Moscow following the attempt on his life
in Groznyy on October 6.

An Interior Méinistry’s Public Relations Center
spokesman told INTERFAX that Shkirko, 48, is the
chief of staff, first deputy commander of the interior
troops.

Shkirko was born in Groznyy. He graduated from the
higher military school in the city of Ordzhonikidze,
now Vladikavkaz. He served in interior troops in the
Baltic republics, in the Urals and in Western Siberia.
He graduated from the General Staff Academy in 1991.

According to the spokesman, Shkirko is an expert in
the problems of the North Caucasus. While first deputy
interior troops commander he often stayed in Groznyy
and knows the current situation in Chechnya well.

Meanwhile, sources in the federal troop command in
Groznyy told INTERFAX that the combined contingent
of federal troops in Chechnya had been renamed into
Russia’s provisional forces in the Chechen republic.
Correspondingly, General Shkirko will enjoy the status
of the commander of these provisional forces.

Maslov Favors Limited Emergency State

LD1210195295 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1712 GMT 12 Oct 95

([FBIS Transcribed Text] GROZNYY, Oct 12 (INTER-
FAX) — Pavel Maslov, deputy commander of Russian
Defense Ministry troops in Chechnya, spoke in favor of
declaring a state of emergency in several regions of the
republic.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

He told INTERFAX that the state of emergency need
not affect the whole of Chechnya. "It will be enough to
declare it in Groznyy and the Achkhoy-Martan region
(on Ingush border — IF Note),”" said Maslov. He said
that the measure is due to bustling terrorist activity in
Groznyy.

According to Maslov, should the state of emergency
be declared it will not hamper but will even facilitate
the work of the Special Supervising Commission. Given
the guarantees we provided to the Dudayev side at the
talks, we will be able to ensure their free movement by
issuing passes, warning check points, providing escort
and security, said Maslov.

He believes that the state of emergency would allow
troops to better tackle the issue of “the illegal armed
groups which Dudayev supporters characterize as the
third force" impeding the talks.

"Under the state of emergency we would have broader
powers in order to take more efficient measures against
such groups,” said Maslov.

Oleg Ivanovich, we received alarming news, reports
from Chechnya that Dzhokhar Dudayev who heads the
defense committee in the self-proclaimed republic of

of terrorism have previously been committed, including

who has still not regained consciousness according to
the most recent reports and is in a serious condition
in the Burdenko hospital. And this is despite the fact
that only a few weeks ago Arkadiy Volskiy, a member
our delegation at the talks, said in this studio that our
mothers, the soldiers’ mothers could sleep soundly. And
now what? Will there be war again?

{Lobov] I do not think so. If you look at the history of
the talks and the conclusion of military agreements, you
will not see a major role for Dudayev there. He did not
sign any document. And he also made many comments
which contradicted accords, and repeatedly. Completely
different people signed them. But unfortunately, of what
was signed, virtually no point in the agreement was
implemented. There have always been veasons why
the timetable for the surrender of weapous should be

why there should be some question of a pause, and so

his statement. A time when the main political figure,
and military figure, General Romanov is unconscious.

(Kiselev}] But nevertheless, the political situation and
the military situation are probably escalating because
reports are coming in every day about our troops being
shelled, our soldiers are being killed. One was killed
again last night. Who does this escalation of the situation
suit? IZVESTIYA put forward two versions yesterday.
The first was that the escalation of the situation suited
the Chechen leaders who are losing popularity on the
eve of the elections which are still planned. Do you
agree with this version?

{Lobov] I think that it is difficult to agree because there
has been no opinion poll on the popularity of the interim
government and the Committee of National Accord.
And we cannot speak about the people’s opinion. It
is better to speak about the opinions of individual
leaders and political figures. It is no secret that there
are several groups in the Chechen republic and several
political leaders who claim the role of leader. You
know that Khasbulatov has now actively joined in the
political process as has the former first secretary of the
Chechen-Ingush republic and the current party regional

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 47

committee, Zavgayev. He is also the chairman of the
Supreme Soviet — a session was held recently and the
Chechen supreme soviet was again set up. These are
the real political figures who enjoy some confidence
among part of the population. I think that there is
also some confidence in Salambek Khadzhiyev’s interim
government and the Committee of National Accord. But
no one has the majority, just as Dudayev does not have
the support of the majority of the population.

[Kiselev] There is another version which IZVESTIYA

{Lobov] I disagree completely. I think this version bears
very little resemblance to what is happening today.
This might have been the case in 1991, or in 1990,
when weapons started to go missing. The situation is
completely different now.

[Kiselev] [passage omitted) The irreconcilable
Chechens say: We will commit a number of acts
of terrorism, then we will blow up an atomic power

successfully, they put General Romanov out of action

[Lobov] The problem of terrorism exists throughout the

to seizing hostages, they then went on to aeroplanes and
they have no gone on to hospitals and so on. So the dy-
namics may not be favorable but nevertheless this route
is one without prospects. On the one hand Dudayev is
proclaiming, including during his last interview, that he
will never stoop to terrorism whereas we know of his
Statements and calls for Russia to be burnt down in an
atomic fire. What is that if not a call to terrorism. So
we must be ready to deflect attempts at terror, including
atomic terror. There is a statement by the Ministry of
Atomic Power Engineering on this subject. Of course
the law and order bodies are taking measures to prevent
acts of terrorism and no one should hope that terrorists
will go unpunished. I think that even Basayev should
understand that a second attempt to get out by talks
will, of course, end in failure for him.

[Kiselev] Well, the experience to date of combating
terrorism is on the whole not thoroughly in our favor.
Romanov is in hospital, and Budennovsk ...

of the funds is being guaranteed. In this case the funds
are getting through in just one or two days or three at

As for whether much or little has been invested there, it
has so far been very little. At the same time the life of

(FBIS Translated Text) Urus-Martan Rayon, Groznyy
— All the approaches to the village of Roshni-Chu
in Urus-Martan Rayon, the village hit by the recent
Russian air raids, have been blocked off by federal
troops. “We have an order not to admit journalists,”

50 RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS

men from the Irkutsk OMON [Special Purpose Police

Nevertheless, I managed to get through to the scenes of
the recent bombardments. The air raid left one street
almost completely destroyed and around 60 families
homeless. As I was told at the Urus-Martan Rayon
Internal Affairs Department [ROVD], eight civilians
died. They included a six-year old girl. There were
19 people injured. “There are 15 air-raid victims in
hospital today, seven of them in a serious condition.

whatever happens many of them will be crippled. At
least two will have to have limbs amputated. Come
to the wards yourself and look at these ‘gunmen’ —
the majority of them are children of preschool age and
women," Ruslan Visarigov, chief doctor at the Urus-
Martan hospital, said.

This rayon was traditionally considered a stronghold of
the anti-Dudayev opposition. During Dudayev’s rule the
rayon’s inhabitants refused to recognize the authority of
the first Chechen president. It was here in summer 1994
that Beslan Gantemirov, now mayor of Groznyy, formed
his armed detachments to combat Dudayev.

What Dudayev failed to do, the Russian Army has
managed to do for him. During the first Chechen
president’s rule the inhabitants of the rayon refused to
hoist Dudayev’s banners over the rural soviet despite
coercion. Today, however, those same villagers are
voluntarily raising banners which they detested a year
ago in the most prominent places in their villages. Today
the green banner with a wolf, the symbol of the Chechen
resistance, can be seen everywhere and the walls of
buildings are daubed with the inscription: "We're with
you, Dzhokhar. Freedom or death!"

"In the elections people will vote for anyone who
is against Russia. When the Russian troops arrived |
convinced people not to be afraid of them. I said that
we had nothing to fear from our Russian Army. Now I
am ashamed to look my fellow-countrymen in the face,”
Eduard Mashakayev, superintendent of the Urus-Martan
ROVD, told your IZVESTIYA correspondent.

The bombing of Roshni-Chu was a marvelous free gift
to the supporters of war until victory in the Dudayev
camp — Russia is managing to turn the inhabitants of
recently loyal rayons against itself and the rebellious
president is being given new supporters.

Dudayev’s State Committee for the Defense of Chech-
nya has stated that, because of the air raid on the
Chechen village of Roshni-Chu, the talks and imple-
mentation of the agreement on military questions are
being suspended until the arrival of international ob-

13 October 1995

servers and UN peacekeeping forces. But Chechen inde-
pendence rallies are continuing in Groznyy. The demon-
strators managed to raise Dudayev’s flag over the ruins
of the president’s palace. Has everything come “full cir-
cle"?

Finance Minister Restates Stance on 1996 Budget

[FBIS Translated Text] Finance Minister Vladimir Pan-
skov said Wednesday [11 October] that the government
will submit a draft federal budget with what are now
well-known base indicators — expenditure of 414.421
trillion rubles [R], revenue of R332.573 trillion, and a
maximum deficit of R81.847 — to a plenary session
of the State Duma. The budget is based on a fore-
cast monthly inflation figure of 1.2 percent and GDP
of R2,100 trillion.

Deputies’ serious criticism of the government's draft
1996 federal budget over the last few weeks has failed
to put a damper on its desire to bring the batfle to a
victorious conclusion.

During a special briefing on Wednesday given by
Vladimir Panskov, who had returned from Washington a
day ahead of schedule evidently to properly prepare for

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

the battle with the Duma scheduled for 13 October, the
minister talked about the positions that the government
will be upholding.

“The government has no intention of recalculating the
draft 1996 federal budget in response to the parliamen-
tary commission’s request and the decision adopted by
participants in the State Duma budget hearings that the
monthly inflation level be raised from 1 to 3-5 percent,"
the minister said, thus once again reinforcing the grav-
ity of his position.

Panskov did not, however, rule out a compromise
solution, which presupposes a rise in the level of
inflation to 2 percent, but only “if deputies decide
to set up a conciliation commission in line with the
recommendations of the State Duma’s Budget and
Finance Committee, adopted by deputies Saturday 7
October."

According to the minister, the scenario proposed by the
budget committee, which presupposes constructive joint
work by deputies and the government on the proposed
budget projections, is also acceptable and gives rise to
hope that the budget will be adopted before the current
Federal Assembly’s term of office expires.

The minister confirmed the feasibility of the Finance
Ministry’s calculations based on 1-percent inflation,
stressing that computations based on a higher level
of inflation would entail insuperable difficulties with
the implementation of the economic reform policy in
Russia.

Should that happen, the finance minister pointed out, all
the government’s efforts during the talks with the Paris
and London Clubs to reschedule the former USSR’s
foreign debt would be “nullified.”

Agriculture Minister Cited on Grain Harvest, Prices

MMI1210134895 Moscow SELSKAYA ZHIZN
in Russian 12 Oct 95 pl

(Report by Tamara Lezhnina: "Figures for First Phase.
State Has So Far Only Bought 500,000 Tonnes of Grain.
But There Should Be a Minimum of 8.6 Million Tonnes
in the Homeland’s Granaries"]

(FBIS Translated Text] Will we have our own grain?
We can report that at a recent press conference Russian
Minister of Agriculture and Food A.G. Nazarchuk
replied overall in the affirmative to this burning question
that concerns everybody. True, the expected grain yield
of 65-66 million tonnes is the lowest since 1965. But
the demand for bread grain of 24-25 million tonnes can
still be satisfied from domestic production.

RUSSIA NATIONAL AFFAIRS 51

The agriculture department head did not conceal how
hard it would be to accomplish this task. The federal
food stocks have as yet only received 500,000 of a
planned total of 8.6 million tonrss of grain. The reason
for this is that the Ministry of A; ‘culture and Food and
the Federal Food Corporation are short of funds.

It is very likely that the peasants’ crops wili be bought
up by commercial structures, from which it will prove
necessary to acquire the grain later at world prices.
It seems that it will prove expensive to obtain the
necessary capital from commercial banks at a high
interest rate, which threatens to push the price of a
loaf of bread up to 5,000 rubles [R]. Therefore, it is
considered at the Ministry of Agriculture and Food that
the government should defray a portion of the outlay
and could arrange this credit as a budget loan at an
annual rate of 60 percent.

One thing is clear: We cannot run away from resolving
these problems. Either we purchase grain from our own
commodity producers today, or tomorrow we will not
only be forced to buy it from commercial structures,
but from abroad, the minister emphasized. At the same
time it cannot be ruled out that our own grain may in
the meantime go abroad, to Poland, which has already
requested it from us on more than one occasion. A.G.
Nazarchuk linked the present situation in the grain
market with the departure from the traditional method
of financing the agro-industrial complex, which has
had a negative effect by weakening the rural areas’
materials and technical base. Such a policy threatens
the agro-industrial complex with serious problems next
year as well. Of 12 million hectares of winter crops,
only 1.5 million have received fertilizer; what is more,
a significant proportion of them have been sown with
nongrade seeds.

It is simply essential for the rural areas to have
credit of the magnitude of R20-22 trillion and at an
affordable interest rate. It is in these precise terms
that the Agriculture and Food Ministry leader puts the
question. Otherwise, rebuilding capital assets, acquiring
equipment and other absolutely essential resources, and
construction work is out of the question.

In reply to a question about how he sees the land market,
Nazarchuk said: Until Russia becomes a stable state
with a solid legal basis, the question of the free purchase
and sale of land is premature.
