FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

It is generally known that the main reason for excessive
energy consumption lies in the deformed structure of
the Ukrainian economy, which preconditions its loss
making. The adequate structural changes that are aimed
at removing such deformations, inherited from the old
economic system, have been outlined in the program as
one of the strategic directions of the activities. At the
same time one should understand that deep structural
changes will take more than one year to implement,
and they also require astronomical financial expenditure.
Therefore we will work actively on what is possible and
necessary already today.

We can identify three components of this issue. First,
fuller use of Ukraine’s own energy resources, which can
satisfy up to 20 percent of the need for natural gas and
up to 13 percent of the need for oil. To do that there
are plans to expand geological exploration, in particular
on the Black Sea-Azov Sea shelf, and to use fuller the
deposits that have been decommissioned earlier.

As for the increase in production of coal, reserves of
which meet the needs of our economy, besides purely
technical and economic measures there are plans to
reform the management system of the industry and to
restructure actively its enterprises.

There is another unforgivably forgotten reserve — the
use of local fuel resources, for example, peat. While
thinking in global terms one should not forget about
what is immediately under one’s feet.

Second, search for additional financial sources of pay-
ment for the import of energy carriers and improvement
of the organization of mutual relations between the par-
ticipants of this process. To rely only on foreign cred-
its, which are so far sufficient for just about payment
for energy carriers, would be shortsighted and danger-
ous: these credits can disappear tomorrow. It is also ex-
tremely unprofitable to live in debt because these debts
do not just have to be returned but one also has to pay
large sums to service them. Hence it is absolutely im-
perative that one should earn as much as possible hard
currency by any means. This is one of the main motives
to stimulate production of export worthy and import re-
placing production.

To improve the balance of payments the government
has drawn up a new system of economic and organi-
zational relations connected with gas deliveries, settling
for it and its transit through Ukrainian territory. It will
be based essentially on centralization and providing con-
sumers and sellers with the freedom to chose relations
between themselves. In other words, within this sys-
tem the state will stop being a monopolist that was in
charge of everything and that almost completely was
responsible for all the debts. The establishment and de-

UKRAINE 71

velopment of market relations between the suppliers and
consumers of electric energy and competition between
generating companies will benefit from the restructured
management in the power engineering sector, which is
basically already completed. The government, together
with the Supreme Council, will draw up a draft national

energy program.

Third, with regard to the importance of the above-
mentioned tasks, energy conservation will be the main
focus of this work. This will be implemented both
through long-term future measures in the form of re-
structuring as well as through operational and organi-
zatioxO To the latter, we include the setting up of ac-
counting methods and control over the use of energy
resources in the productive and domestic spheres by
means of introducing relevant systems and instruments.
The tariff policy should become an active instrument
in the introduction of the conservation program. Enter-
prises are entirely responsible for paying for energy,
and tariffs and prices for fuel, housing, and utilities are
gradually getting closer to their real value.

Obviously this is an unpopular step, but it is necessary
and, what is more, inevitable. It is better to pay more
for heat and light than not to have it at all. In addition,
as I have already said, the government will make use
of warning measures mainly through the mechanism
of targeted assistance to the most vulnerable strata of
the population. The structural and industrial policy is
important not only from the point of view of the fuel
and energy complex. It is an all-embracing problem that
determines the future of our economy. From what has
been said I would like to focus on several aspects that
describe the unconventional vision of resolving them as
seen by the government.

What I mean is to transfer the emphasis onto the
sphere that for a long time seemed to remain in the
shadows of powerful basic sectors: the light and food
industries. In the meantime these sectors that make the
final product have a considerable export potential, work
directly for the consumer market, ensure a quick return
on their capital, provide considerable revenues into
the budget, and contribute to lowering unemployment.
Unfortunately, such potential advantages as yet remain
unfulfilled. Moreover, statistics prove that precisely
light industry and the output of consumer goods have
suffered the biggest decline. In connection with this we
set ourselves the tasks of not only stopping the rate of
decline but also to turn these sectors into locomotives
that would speed up the process of pulling the economy
out of the crisis. World experience is proof of the
expediency of this approach.

72 UKRAINE

Here we must also include those sectors that used to be
mentioned in government documents at best through a
colon and which in terms of their economic efficiency
now possess one of the most important places. I am re-
ferring to tourism, which, with relatively small expen-
ditures, is capable of consistently and quickly providing
considerable funds, including hard currency; new jobs;
and gives an impulse to a range of related sectors, par-
ticularly with regard to the infrastructure. The inclusion
of such spheres among the government's priorities ob-
viously does not mean that attention to other sectors
is being weakened, especially the basic and scientifi-
Cally intensive sectors as well as machine and instru-
ment building, metallurgy, the chemical industry, trans-
port, and so on. The program mentions this in detail. In
the agro-industrial sector of the economy, which gener-
ates one third of national income, the government will
promote the creation of new socioeconomic relations
and forms of management of the market type. At the
center of this work are the interests of agricultural pro-
ducers and the need to raise motivation to increase and
efficiently use the productive potential. We regard !and
and economic reform and increasing support for private
farming as an important precondition for the formation
of a mixed economy in the agricultural sphere.

Cooperative processes, the organizational and techno-
logical integration of output, procurement, transport, and
sales will continue to develop. To reduce and then in
the future overcome dependancy on imports we will im-
plement the national program of developing agricultural
machine-building. The production of our own mineral
fertilizers and pesticides will be speeded up.

A combination of free and regulated price formation will
be the backbone of the price policy in the countryside.
The system of state contracts for some produce required
to satisfy the needs of the budgetary sphere will be
preserved. The produce purchase and sale infrastructure
will be improved along with the formation of specialized
markets of grain, sugar, vegetable oil, etc. The specific
features of the agrarian sector, as a seasonal branch
with the slowed-up circulation of invested funds, will
be taken into account, to improve financial and credit
relations in the sector. Export potentials of the sector
will be used more effectively.

Taking into account the special urgency of the problem,
I would like to dwell on the progress of the formatioa
of state grain resources and on measures taken by
the government to accelerate the process. This year
[1995], the bulk yield of grain crops in Ukraine is
expected to be about 37.3 million tonnes; in other
words, it is expected at the level of last year’s harvest.
As regards wheat, which is the main food crop, 16.4
million tonnes of wheat have been harvested; that is

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

26 percent more than last year [1994]. However, it is
better to calculate the amount of grain that has already
been stored. The government believes that the volume
of grain produced, including wheat, makes it possible
to form resources required to meet the needs of the
population, set up a state reserve, and form a seed
stock. As at 6 October, 4.7 million tonnes of grain were
procured under the state contract. The government has
allocated funds to pay for the 832,000 tonnes of grain
supplied by farms for storage under the responsibility of
the state. An additional reserve for grain procurement
is being effected — purchases of grain for the money
allocated by local authorities. Thus, over 300,000 tonnes
of grain have been purchased. In line with the decision
by the Cabinet of Ministers, the state will continue to
purchase grain at the expense of redeeming the budget
loan — as of 1 September, the debt stood at 20.6 trillion
karbovantsi; and also at the expense of redeeming the
loans issued by credit banks and at the expense of the
procurement organizations’ own circulating assets.

The government’s resolution on measures to develop
the market of agricultural produce in Ukraine should
play a special role. Under the resolution, the procedure
of introducing quotas and licenses is not applied to
the produce sold through agricultural exchanges; the
provision of rolling stock to transport this produce will
be a priority. The state will take part in trading sessions
on a par with other buyers. Besides, the procedure
speeds up the process of obtaining funds by farms,
which are required to finish autumn field work.

In present-day conditions, foreign economic activities
acquire ever greater importance as a system-forming
element. A whole number of difficulties and obstacles
are still to be overcome in this sphere. The foreign trade
and inter-state economic relations infrastructure, which
ceased to exist with the disintegration of the USSR,
is being set up virtually from scratch. The majority,
if not all of our exporters, turned out unprepared to
act independently on the world market with its strong
competition.

The government has to fill these gaps fast, because only
an increase in exports can somehow make up for the
losses caused by the economic crisis, particularly the de-
Cline in output and the shrinking of the domestic market.
This does not mean that exports will be given free rein.
We will work to improve the product structure of our
exports, moving away from the domination of raw ma-
terials to the sale of finished products and overcoming
the barterization of foreign trade. Here we are already
witnessing a positive trend. The measures envisaged to
stimulate the process include the implementation of a
long-term comprehensive program for providing sup-

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

port to exporters, due to be approved by the beginning
of next year.

Another and no less important aspect of the work
that is strategically aimed at improving the balance
of payments is, we believe, the implementation of
a flexible import policy. It should be pursued by
means of economically justified tariff regulation and the
introduction of specific, combined, and serious import
duties. Ukraine as a sovereign state has every right
to take these and other measures, which, incidentally,
conform to universally accepted international standards.

In line with the task of pursuing a flexible import policy,
work is in progress to step up the output of import-
replacing products. The fact that, despite our poverty,
hard currency is being wasted on noncritical imports —
that is, things we can do without or produce ourselves —
is peculiar not only from the point of complex economic
calculations but even from the point of common sense.

The program presents in detail our view on the status
quo, the tasks, and the prospects of development in the
field of foreign economic relations, including those with
the CIS and Russia. While we are in favor of building
economic bridges with all countries, we believe that this
process cannot remain a one-way route. The government
will stand firmly and consistently in favor of symmetry
and equality in foreign economic relations.

Another important section of the program has to do with
institutional transformations, the ownership reform, the
development of enterprise, and provisions for the man-
ageability of the state sector of the economy. It en-
visages better organization of privatization processes,
meaning that they should not develop on their own. The
restructuring of ownership is aimed at finding an effi-
cient Owner, increasing competition among producers,
and improving the feedback from production in general
on this basis. In the first instance, the acceleration of pri-
vatization will affect enterprises that bear no strategic
significance for the country’s economic development.
Their privatization is to be completed in the first half

UKRAINE 73

of next year. Here, the most burning problem is the
privatization of unfinished construction projects, where
enormous furds remain frozen. We are ready to hand
over a number of such projects at a symbolic price to
those who will be able to bring them to completion.

The privatization of medium-sized and large enterprises
requires particular consideration. It is envisaged to pro-
ceed by sale of shares in open joint-stock companies es-
tablished on the basis of these enterprises’ assets. This
will enable people to use their vouchers issued for the
purpose of privatization, purchase of property, and in
compensation. To protect state interests, the privatiza-
tion of enterprises that are of special significance to
economy and security will proceed according to individ-
ual schedules. By the end of this year, we will improve
the system of control over the involvement of financial
agents in the process of privatization, specifying leg-
islative grounds for their establishment and operation.
We will consider the possibility of extending the con-
trol powers of local authorities.

By the end of this year, Ukraine will have largely
formed a mixed economic system, along with the
necessary means of state influence on its operation.
While we are focusing attention on the long-term
problems of national importance, ours is not a bird's eye
view of the subject, overlooking the everyday problems
and concerns of individual sectors, work collectives,
citizens, and their families. The logical structure of
government activities also envisages urgent day-to-day
measures required to ensure the normal functioning of
the national economy as well as housing and utilities
during the autumn and winter period; food provision;
and the regular payment of wages, pensions, student
grants, and cash benefits. The government regards the
current state of economy and the actions planned as
the basis for its policies in the humanitarian, science,
and technology spheres. The tasks envisaged here, as in
other areas, are strictly coordinated with the actual state
resources available.

74 WESTERN REGION

Belarus

President Lukashenka Addresses Trade Unionists

WS1310103195 Minsk BELAPAN in English
0806 GMT 13 Oct 95

(FBIS Transcribed Text] "Administration in the Repub-
lic of Belarus will be on toughening trend," President
Alyaksandr Lukashenka said while speaking at the Sec-
ond Congress of the FTUB [Federation of Belarusian
Trade Unions). He said, however, that he did not pretend
to the role of the author of a dictatorship. Admitting the
fact that in the country there are different branches of
power — the Supreme Soviet, the Constitutional Court,
the press, as the so called ‘fourth power’, and even trade
unions to a certain degree (as five million people are
behind them), Alyaksandr Lukashenka reminded those
present that “all these branches of power are attributed
to the trunk of one tree and people elected the President
as this trunk.” He also stressed that he did not want to
turn the Federation of Trade Unions into a pro-President
organisation. However, the consolidation of all forces of
society is vitally necessary at the present time. Only un-
der this condition the Republic can be lifted out of crisis
levels.

In his speech the President touched on his last decrees
and orders concerning the abolition of social privileges
and pensions for working pensioners. The head of state
tried to persuade the delegates of the Congress that
"economic progress is impossible without overcoming
social parasitic smugness,” and that "we must not turn
the state into a social security organisation.” In addition,
the President stressed, the initiators of these decrees
were common people themselves who demanded that
young people should be given an opportunity to find
jobs. The President admitted that issuing these and other
unpopular decrees he may have intruded upon others’
spheres of activities. But nobody but he can take upon
himself the solution of pressing problems. As for the
social privileges, according to Alyaksandr Lukashenka,
basically high-ranking bureaucrats, in particular, the
MP’s of the old parliament, enjoyed them. That is
why after this presidential decree appeared, they started
fighting against the President so zealously.

Mr. Lukashenka also dwelt on relationships between
him and the Constitutional Court. He said that on
the morning October 12 he had a conversation with
the Constitutional Court Chairman during which they
reached a mutual understanding. As for the activities of
the Free Trade Union of Belarus, which were suspended
by Decree No. 336, the President’s position remains
unwavering here: "I will not allow them to destabilize
the situation in the country. 100 metro drivers must not
disturb the 2-million city."

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

Speaking about problems concerning the financial
sphere he said that at the present time three special
commissions are inspecting the activities of the Na-
tional Bank, the Savings Bank and commercial banks.
The President promised that in the near future large
sections of the public will be able to get acquainted
with the results of their work. In conclusion the
President shared his opinion about the Programme of
the Economic Development of the Republic in the
period until the year 2000, which is being worked out
now. Mr. Lukashenka considers that such a programme
should have ideological and political sections. "Why
have we given up the state ideology?” the President
asked. "Before January 1, 1996 the government plans
to work out an integral and complex programme to
outline ideas according to which, in my opinion, society
must live,” Mr. Lukashenka said. But he immediately
added that the draft of this document will also appear
in the press and be discussed by the general public.

KRASNAYA ZVEZDA has written about the incident
several times. And now, speaking on national television
recently, Alyaksandr Lukashenka has stated that the
results of a medical examination of the remains of the
dead pilots which will become known in about three
weeks’ time should clear up the matter completely. But
he said that it is already clear that the incident was

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

transliterated], commander of the republic’s air defense.
The president and the commander in chief of the
Belarusian armed forces were in Moldova at the time
and he was not informed of the incident. [Kovalev report
ends]

Specialist’s Commentary

Since the tragedy involving the South Korean Boeing
whose “deviation” from its route ended 1 September
1983 over Sakhalin, and Matthias Rust’s landing in Red
Square, the incident in the Belarusian sky leading to the
death of two Americans in the car of the air balloon
that was shot down is perhaps the first serious test of
air sovereignty in the post-Soviet space.

The U.S. side’s responses are understandable — the in-
cident has been called an extremely serious occurrence.
Our own mass media, grown skilled in journalists’ snap
investigations, did not remain aloof either, stating that
back in May the “chief Belarusian border guard” had
received a letter announcing the planned flight by for-
eign air balloons into the republic. I do not know how
reliable this fact is but as a specialist I can assert that
all airborne vehicles — from air balloons to ballistic
missiles — cross the border not on the basis of letters
giving advance notification but of applications made in
the approved form and within the deadlines approved
by international law. And it is not the border guards
who should track airborne vehicles, but the air defense
facilities. The long-suffering air defense forces who, if
they shoot something down, are abused on all sides but
if they do not shoot it down far even worse. After the
Rust story a dozen generals were dismissed.

But let us return to the events in Belarus.

It will be annoying if the investigation into this incident
takes place only under pressure from a “great power.”
Of course much will depend on the investigation com-
mission. Will its members be able or to be more precise
will they want to clarify the main issues: What applica-
tions were made by the organizers of the international
air balloon competition, “nai safety measures did they
develop and agree with the countries on the route, was
there a radio on board the balloons, and so forth? The
situation is aggravated by the fact that the balloon which
was shot down was by no means the only one there.
A whole squadron crossed Belarus’ air border and the
crews acted in different ways: Some landed after orders
from other helicopters, some flew away across the bor-

But the main question, of course, is whether it was
necessary to open fire. The supporters of a negative
reply assert that the balloon presented no danger.
But what if an aircraft had collided with it, a large

WESTERN REGION 75

aircraft, even, carrying tourists from one of those
civilized foreign countries? Who would have been the
“scapegoat” then? International law has a clear reply
to this question: He who controls the air space. And
control, you will agree, implies interception. And not
only for us. For instance, the Americans controlled
the air in the region of their cruiser Vincennes which,
moreover, was not off U.S. shores but thousands of
miles away, in the Persian Gulf, and instantly shot down
an Iranian airbus taking it for an enemy. More people
died than on the South Korean airliner. And nothing
happened. The Israelis shot down a lost Libyan airliner
over Sinai when the aircraft was turning to correct an
error. And it was shot down in broad daylight, when all
the inscriptions on its chassis could be read.

So that such incidents are by no means our Slav
“prerogative.”

An in-depth and fundamental investigation of all the fac-
tors which led to the emergence and development of the
situation and not just the dramatization of its denoue-
ment are very desirable. That this investigation will be
international is obvious. But will it be independent?

And I should also like an official report to be published
after the investigation and not just that the incident
should be arbitrarily retold by an official anxious, as
often happens, to remaini anonymous.

Moldova

Snegur Opposes Merger With Romania, Joining
NATO

LD1210202495 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1916 GMT 12 Oct 95

[By Gennadiy Temnenkhov]

(FBIS Transcribed Text] BONN, October 12 (Itar-
Tass) — President of Moldova Mircea Snegur, who
is currently on an official visit to Germany, spoke
against Moldova becoming a part of Romania at a press
conference held in Bonn.

“If we talk of economic and cultural integration between
the two friendly states of Romania and Moldova, we are
in favour of such an integration", he said, replying to
an Itar-Tass query. "However, if it concerns a territorial
union with Romania, then our position is clear — the
present state in Europe and principles of security on
the continent are sealed in official documents of the
Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe
(OSCE) and the Paris Charter, and we are in favour of
strict compliance to provisions of these documents”, he
said.

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

76 WESTERN REGION

Moldova does not aspire for entering NATO, Mircea
Snegur hinted. The country’s constitution states that
Moldavia cannot become member of military alliances
and cannot permit the presence of foreign troops in its
territory. At the same time, Moldova was one of the first
to sign NATO’s “partnership for peace" programme,
since it regards that it can prove useful not only in the
sphere of military, but also in economic and political
spheres, the president said.

As far as NATO's eastward expansion plans are con-
cerned, according to the Moldovan leader, “they do not
harm security in Europe", however, they should be sub-
jected to a careful multilateral analysis, seriously taking
into consideration its "Russian component”.

FBIS-SOV-95-198

The Decree envisages a considerable toughening of

Russia is the main market for most Dniester enterprises,

sian credits so badly needed by Dniester, Smirnov said.

Tajikistan

Government Claims Rebels Accelerating Conflict

LD1I210141595 Dushanbe Radio Tajikistan Network
in Tajik 1700 GMT 11 Oct 95

(FBIS Translated Text] At a briefing for local and
foreign correspondents on 11 October, Igor Sattarov,
deputy of the Tajik Ministry of Foreign Affairs Infor-
mation Department, announced the following statement
of the Government of the Republic of Tajikistan:

On 10 October 1995 at 1720 one of the units belonging
to the die-hard armed Tajik opposition group located
in the Afghan village of Barchid fired on a vehicle of
the Khorog border detachment. The vehicle was car-
rying soldiers of the Russian Federation Border Troop
detachment, as well as civilians from the Republic of
Tajikistan. According to preliminary information, five
soldiers died and four others were wounded as a result
of this action.

The Government of Tajikistan has made a strong protest
to the authorities of the Islamic State of Afghanistan
because this provocation was committed from that ter-
ritory. This shameful action also shows the extent to
which the Tajik opposition leaders respect the agree-
ments they have signed with the Government of Tajik-
istan. According to these agreements, peaceful negotia-
tions are recognized as the sole means to attain political
aims. In this respect also, there is no doubt that the
actions undertaken by the opposition are aimed at frus-
trating the crucial and decisive fifth round of inter-Tajik
negotiations.

The leadership of Tajikistan, by expressing its protest
against the violation of the agreements, once again
States its resolve that peace and tranquillity should be
established on the unfortunate Tajik land, and calls on
the Tajik opposition leadership to chose a judicious path,
to halt the senseless bloodshed, and to sit down at the
negotiating table.

There have been increasingly frequent armed actions of
late by the Tajik rebels on Tajik territory, according
to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs employee. These
have entailed the death of two soldiers and the serious
wounding by gunshot of two other Russian soldiers on
28 September in Dushanbe; the hostage taking of four
policemen — whose fate is unknown to this day —
on 9 October in Tajikabad District; and provocative
actions on the Tajik-Afghan border. These all show that
the die-hard groups of the Tajik opposition, despite the
agreement between the Government of Tajikistan and
the leadership of the Tajik opposition, are increasingly
descending down a path of armed terrorism and are
departing far from their political opponents.

Russian Border Guard Commander Threatens
Retaliation

LD1210152795 Dushanbe Radio Tajikistan Network
in Russian 1000 GMT 12 Oct 95

(FBIS Translated Text] After a certain lull the situation
on the Tajik-Afghan border has sharply deteriorated
again. This statement was made at the start of a news
conference given yesterday by Lieutenant General Pavel
Tarasenko, commander of the group of Border Troops
of the Russian Federation in the Republic of Tajikistan,
on the latest provocation by the Tajik armed opposition,
which took place on 10 October and, according to the
latest clarified data, resulted in the killing of seven
Russian border guards.

Pavel Tarasenko drew the attention of the Tajik and
foreign journalists present to reliable facts indicating
the accumulation of large armed forces on the Afghan
border in the Pyandzh and Shuroabad areas covered
by the Moskovskiy border detachment and the Kalai-
Khumb, Vanch, and Yazgulem areas under the Khorog
border detachment.

As the commander announced, the investigation into
the tragic incident, when fire was opened on a vehicle
transporting foodstuffs from one observation point to
another, shows that the militants had prepared for the
attack beforehand, because small-arms and grenade-
launcher fire was carried out simultaneously from 20
points in Afghanistan. It is already the second case since
the beginning of the year when fire was opened against
unarmed groups of border guards.

What happened illustrates clearly once again that there
are some forces in the republic and beyond its bor-
ders who are obstructing in every possible way the
establishment of peace in Tajikistan and whose inter-
ests are served by the maintenance of tension on the
Tajik-Afghan border, the commander of the group of
Russian Border Troops stated. He placed responsibil-
ity for the attack on armed groups of the irreconcilable
armed Tajik opposition and stated emphatically that, if
such acts are repeated, the Russian border guards will
take appropriate measures. In future, if fire is opened on
the border guards from Afghan territory, fire will also
be opened from Tajik territory, Pavel Tarasenko stated.
Afghan border commissioners have been informed of
this.

Concluding the news conference, Lt. Gen. Tarasenko
answered numerous questions from the journalists.

Foreign Minister on Relations With Mideast

{Interview with Foreign Minister Abdulaziz Kamilov by

([FBIS Translated Text} [Abu-Husayn] Some political
circles have remarked on the expansion of Israel's
activities in Asia along the lines of its known activities
in the Horn of Africa. What stage has been reached as
regards your relations with Israel?

{Kamilov] Our relations with Israel are primarily eco-
nomic. We welcomed these relations in view of the his-
toric changes taking place in the Middle East arena. We
also welcomed having an Israeli embassy in Tashkent,
in view of these changes and for the sake of our coun-
try’s economic interests, in addition to what we see hap-
pening, namely Yasir ‘Arafat shaking hands and mak-
ing peace with both Israeli Premier Yitzhaqg Rabin and
Israeli Foreign Minister Shim’on Peres. Therefore, we
welcome this historic step aimed at settling the Arab-
Israeli conflict on the basis of international laws and
principles and resolving an issue which has lasted more
than 40 years. We are also willing to cooperate in the
economic field with all world states and to strengthen
this aspect with any state that wishes to do so.

[Abu-Husayn}] Does this mean that the Republic of
Uzbekistan is giving priority to the economy over
politics?

[Kamilov] Yes. This aspect is at the heart of our foreign
policy's political foundations for numerous reasons, in

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

the first place to guarantee stability — which is a
Strategic aim — in order to establish a democratic state
through a free-market policy, because we believe, not
that democracy is the basis of a good economy, but
rather, that the economy is the first safety valve to
protect any state from threats and to propel it toward
achieving security, stability, and development.

[Abu-Husayn] This principle contradicts, however, what
is being reported about attempts by Iran to polarize the
independent Islamic states of the former Soviet Union
to form an Islamic power in Central Asia.

{[Kamilov) First of all, Iran is a neighbor, in the same
region, and a major state.... Second, one does not choose
one’s neighbors, because the presence of neighbors is
in the hand of God. That is why it is in our interests
to develop relations between Uzbekistan and Iran. With
regard to rumors of the formation of an Islamic axis,
observers can say what they want and are entitled to
indulge in political fantasies. As far as we are concerned
we believe that our foreign policy is based on our
country’s national interests first and foremost.

[Abu-Husayn] What about your relations with the King-
dom of Saudi Arabia?

{[Kamilov] Our relations with Saudi Arabia are good and
based on continued consultations. We have an embassy
in Riyadh, and I intend to make an official visit next
November, for further coordination with officials there
over major issues of common interest, and we hope
that in future we will have special relations. There are
great prospects for cooperation in all fields, political,
economic, cultural, and commercial.

[Abu-Husayn] Among the issues that you addressed
in your meeting with Egyptian Foreign Minister "Amr
Musa was the concern over the Afghanistan problem.
What threats does this problem pose to your country?

[Kamilov] First, there are great threats for the region as
a whole due to the prevailing situation in Afghanistan,
inherited from the first war. Consequently, we do not
consider it Central Asia’s problem only, but a problem
which concerns the whole world. There is for example
the issue of drugs, terrorism, arms, and many other
issues. When I visited a European state [not identified},
experts there said that the chemical analysis of the
drugs in Europe indicate that 70 percent of them
are Afghan-made [sina’ah afghaniyah]. This problem
requires international efforts to combat it.

[Abu-Husayn] Another of the issues you discussed was
how to iron out the obstacles facing the peace process.
Will Uzbekistan use the leverage of its economic
relations with Israel to that effect?

13 October 1995

[Kamilov] We support the Arab position with regard to
resolving the Palestinian issue. It is not possible for us
to have a position different from the world’s position on
the necessity to establish peace and end the conflict. We
started our independent foreign policy four years ago,
and today we reaffirm our participation in international
efforts to achieve a comprehensive and just peace based
on international principles, that is peace for all.

[Abu-Husayn] During his visit to Cairo two years ago,
President Islam Karimov met with the Arab League
secretary general, with a view to boosting relations

ganizations. Soon, Uzbekistan will acquire membership
of the Organization of the Islamic Conference as an ob-
server, because of our Islamic and cultural heritage; we
share many common denominators with the Arab states.
At the same time, we want to improve our relations on
the basis of mutual respect and we declare that our po-
sition and principles are against the control of any state
by any other state by force or other means and that we
are against violence, extremism, and terrorism.

[Abu-Husayn] What is your position regarding Iraq, and

[Kamilov] We do not have any relations with Iraq! That

{[Kamilov] Yes. There are some positive developments

region and uniting the ranks to establish the foundations
of an economic union between Kazakhstan, Uzbekistan,
Kurdistan. We must take advantage of our open borders.
We do not impose any customs tariffs or taxes, in order
to encourage investment and boost economic and social
life. We also attach great importance to the CIS states in
our foreign policy, especially as Russia is a superpower
and our relations with it lasted 70 years, but we prefer
however our relations to be based on mutual respect,
equality, and noninterference in internal affairs. We
also wish to develop our relations with Europe and the
United States.

CENTRAL ASIA 79

Karimov Builds Himself New Summer, Winter
Residences

MM1210102595 Moscow KOMSOMOLSKAYA
PRAVDA in Russian 11 Oct 95 p 3

{Report by Aleksandr Ivanov under "Dacha" rubric:
"Uzbek President Parks Himself Next to Puppets"}

(FBIS Translated Text] Tashkent —- Uzbek President
Islam Karimov’s summer residence near Tashkent has
been erected in an unprecedentedly short space of time.
It is hard to believe that only six months ago there was
no trace of this magnificent three-story residence. Work
went on round the clock in five (!) shifts. Anybody
who has seen this construction site will confirm that
it resembled old movie shots of the construction of the
Dnepr Hydroelectric Power Station. The interior was
finished by Yugoslav builders.

The decorating materials and furniture were obtained
from distant foreign countries. A few minor things were
entrusted to local craftsmen. For example, the crystal
stair balusters were made at Tashkent’s “Mikond" plant.

As of today all that remains to be done at the estate is to
apply the finishing touches: The pond with swans and
a footbridge over it is not functioning at the moment.
Some cages of the "domestic" zoo are also empty. For
example, the cage of the spiral-horned goat. But this is
not the builders’ fault: It is not easy to catch such goats
because there are only a handful of them left in the wild.

Meanwhile a plot for the president’s winter residence
has also been selected — a lovely children’s park in
downtown Tashkent. Another hitch emerged here — in
the form of the adjacent puppet theater. It was built with
the intention that after a show children would run around
in the park and play on the swings and merry-go-rounds.
Somebody advised the house builders to demolish the
puppet theater completely. But then they thought better
of it and erected a tall solid fence near the theater,
cordoning off what is now the president’s park. Evil
tongues, however, are inclined to consider the fence
a forgivable architectural excess at such an important
facility.

FBIS-SOV-95-198

lishments and officials must act within the framework of
the law in effect. According to the Armenian legislation,
Ministry of Justice or any other state establishment or
any Official are not entitled to decide whether to allow or

Minister added that from available documents of ARF-
Dashnaktsutyun it is clear that foreigners-members of

[Shevardnadze] Thank you. I have always treated this
newspaper with respect not only for its bold and inde-
pendent judgments, but also — which, to my mind, is
most important of all — for the accuracy and exactness
of assessments that do not mislead the reader and pro-
vide him with correct information about events taking
place in our hard days so as to steer people’s attitudes
and views to the choice of democratic principles. In this
connection, I can recall August 1991, October 1993...
I can recall acrimonious critical publications, including
against the democratic forces as well. This is, however,
natural in our conditions: A surgeon, too, involuntar-
ily causes pain to the patient, but without this he cannot
cure him and bring him back to life. Therefore, I wish to
congratulate NEZAVISIMAYA [GAZETA] on its sal-
vation and preservation, and I am counting on having,
as diplomats say, our mutually beneficial cooperation
continue...

{Broladze] It is not by chance that we have come to you
again precisely at this moment. The point is that when
the Constitution of Georgia was adopted there was a
general feeling that political life in the country started
to regain the long-awaited harmony. And then, all of

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

a sudden — a terrorist act, an attempt on the head of
state’s life! What happened?

[Shevardnadze] Do you know what the difference is
between normal, benevolent people and their opposites?
A normal person seeks order as a natural state. He
detests chaos — an accomplice of evil. Just as night
shrouds crime, a state of permanent anarchy, bloodshed,
and conflicts, on a varying scale, provide a very
convenient backdrop and safe cover for social outrages,
mean intrigues, robberies, and glaring and disgusting
insinuations, the logic of which can never be understood
by a civilized person. Please excuse me, but I would

(Shevardnadze] I recently signed an edict abolishing the

to an era of legalized gangsterism, which started several
years ago. Some people wanted to legitimize this organ-
ization and tried to force such a decision on me, so as
to commit large-scale crimes using "state interests and
needs" as a shield. A mafia of a completely new type
was molded in Georgia, which brought together peo-
ple with completely dissimilar philosophies — former
communist apparatchiks; leaders of the criminal world;
the new military, both high- and low-ranking; and some
others. They found a common language, based on an
immoral aspiration to profiteer from a unique oppor-
tunity — to make fortunes off the country’s hardships
and to use any chance for social looting. Well indeed,
happily seizing any opportunity, they have been shame-
lessly picking these “apples of death" with impunity for
several years. “Mkhedrioni" (although it should be ad-
mitted that many members of this paramilitary organ-
ization honestly fulfilled their duty by defending the
territorial integrity of the country) has become a hide-
out for criminals — including those who can knot a
neat tie and talk big about democracy and dictatorship,
at the same time encouraging gangsterism in its most
undisguised form. I wish to inquire: Why should Jaba
loseliani — whom I, alas, once entrusted to head the
Georgian delegation at the talks with the Abkhazian side
— suddenly need to arrange a sinister hiding place in
his private office, located within the premises occupied
by the government and the head of state’s staff? Why
should he keep there machine guns, thousands of bul-
lets, electric-shock torture devices, and hundreds of am-
poules of narcotic substances? After all, this is no arms

CAUCASUS 81

depot or hospital! And why did he oppose the idea of
the federal structure of Georgia so ardently? Was it be-
cause this could prevent his “protigis” from indulging in
large-scale racketeering? I] am sure he would not have
protested had I appointed his people as my envoys in
the provinces!

When the Constitution, envisioning a strong presidential
power, was approved and passed by the parliament —
with the support of even some opposition forces that did
not refuse our offer to cooperate — the deputies were
on their feet applauding this victory. A feeling of joy
and relief swept everyone who did not fear his past or
future. The only people not applauding were either our
orthodox political opponents, who voted "against" out of
conviction but who are not involved in any state or other
crimes, or those who have a trail of criminal misdeeds
behind them. These are not peacocks, who take pride in
the marvelous feathers of their tails. Those tails cannot
be exposed, but it is hard to hide them, especially if the
“tail” is rather long. Therefore, these pseudo-politicians
took the adoption of the Constitution as a great threat.
They came to realize that the trap had started to unload
its spring and was about to shut. In addition, it turned
out that the good-for-nothing “professionals” from the
security service, who missed the mark on 29 August —
those on whom the state had been counting for the past
three years — were the main protectors of the mafia and
an integral part of it.

{Broladze] It turns out that even you made a mistake,
despite your experience...

{Shevardnadze} After I came back from Moscow and
lent a helping hand to my people, I was sincere in my
readiness to be useful to people, to bring relief, and
to restore the good reputation of the country. Everyone
knows what the legacy was thai I received. Possibly,
however, I experienced good feelings toward some
people who seemed loyal and was unwatchful in my
trust in them.

[Broladze} Igor Giorgadze, former head of the Georgian
Security Service, declared a political and psychological
war against you, blaming you for very serious things.
How do you feel about this, and why, in your opinion,
despite the sanction given by Georgia’s General Prose-
cutor, does Giorgadze feel if not wonderful, then rather
at ease in Moscow?

[Shevardnadze] Let us start with the accusations. It
seems that I personally own two oil wells in Kazakhstan.
I can probably feel rather offended: Why only two?
Does that implies I am inept at business? Joking apart,
however, it is very easy to find out what I actually
own, and such a primitive and straightforward method
of discreditation only shows that the criminal is in

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

82 CAUCASUS

agony and is clutching at straws. Igor Panteleymonovich
(Giorgadze], better than anyone else, realizes that he is
doomed and is aware of how much evil was done by
him.

He will go down in the annals of Georgian history as
the spiritual successor to Cain, the first murderer in the
history of mankind. Therefore, he is trying to break out
of the legal trap in which he has found himself through
his own fault. If he is alleging that Shevardnadze has
several other passions such as, say, selling weapons to
Iran or regularly extracting millions of dollars from the
International Monetary Fund’s safes to somehow pull
through the election, then, accordingly, he expects to
make a fairly strong impression — strong enough for
the Lord and justice to forgive all his wrongdoings right
away and then to deal immediately with Shevardnadze.
If | am found guilty — this I declare for the first time
on the pages of NEZAVISIMAYA GAZETA — I am
prepared to be held responsible by the people. This is
neither acting, nor posing, I assure you. It is a chance to
prove once again my resoluteness to participate in the
democratic transformation to the end, for principled, not
personal considerations, or, let's say, not for reasons of
political egoism. I am prepared for this. As for Igor
Giorgadze, a terrorist on an international scale and,
unquestionably, an agent for a bloodthirsty, I would
say, ideology, a dismal end awaits him in any case.
This depends not on whether corpus delicti is found
in Shevardnadze’s actions. Because when one and the
same person has staged at least five attempts on people's
lives, of which three have ended in homicide, and while
this person, being the security service head of the whole
State, acts, though crudely and primitively, sending
virtually the same gang of assassins for different people,
but at the same time, with some mystical and demoniac
ruthlessness, all of these cannot remain a concern of
one single country. The prosecutor general’s warrant
to arrest an out-and-out criminal — no less a coward
than he is a criminal (at least, in his appearance on
Russian television he iooked frightened and pitiful) —-
as well as the list of his crimes, proven by investigation
and indisputable, cry for justice and common sense:
The assassin and traitor Giorgadze must be delivered
up to justice. I am sure that the Russian authorities will
never help a criminal, especially one such as this, escape
punishment. To the same degree, however, I am not sure
that his former colleagues in the KGB of the USSR will
deny him their assistance. With a still greater degree of
certainty, | can maintain that Russian politicians of a
communist persuasion, for whom Giorgadze and his like
used to be pivotal figures in launching destabilization
trends in Georgia, will go out of their way to see
to it that no court in the worid passes sentence on
Giorgadze. The obscene political storerooms of the

FBIS-SOV-95-198
13 October 1995

Bolshevist associations hold too many things that can
arouse the indignation of the world public.

According to the information at my disposal, another
“secret service man,” Timur Khacheshvili — he was
the deputy head of the security service — when interro-
gated, let slip the names of a number of Russian politi-
cians involved in organizing the 29 August terrorist act
and other crimes.

The last of the exposed crimes of Giorgadze is no less
blasphemous and cynical than the others: On board
the plane by which Generals Giya Karkarashvili and
Paata Datuashvili were going to Moscow, a bomb was
planted. Had this not been revealed in time, all of
the people on board would have been dead. After that
Giorgadze decided to do away with the two directly
in Moscow. Datuashvili was killed, and Karkarashvili
was crippled. That was revenge for the fact that, a year
and a half before, then-Defense Minister Karkarashvili
told him the truth: Giorgadze had clearly evaded combat
engagements on the territory of Abkhazia, and it was he
who withdrew his 450-strong detachinent from combat
action, leading it through the Svanetian Mountains at
a time when Sukhumi was bleeding under a fearsome
barrage of hundreds of guns. Thus, Karkarashvili’s
discontent is easy to understand.

I do not find it possible to say anything concrete about
the citizens of other states, but regarding Giorgadze,
and the problem on the whole, the point is as follows.
As I see it, the proponents of conservative political
views, positions, conceptions, and doctrines that are
dying off, and which once seemed absurd, are still alive
and kicking in all the provinces and countries of the
former CIS. It is only by tradition that Moscow remains
a magnet for these forces, while Russian communists
and socialists have kept the ranks of the leading and
guiding force [the Communist Party]. Accordingly, they
still remain the main protectors of those who share their
views and directions.

I believe that an escape plan for “their people” to be
guarded safely in the event the plot was frustrated had
been worked out in advance.

{Broladze] You are being accused of doing away with
your rivals, and carrying out a wide-ranging purge under
the banner of cracking down on the criminal mafia clans
in order to secure yourself a favorable alignment of
forces in the elections...

{Shevardnadze} Among all those considered to be my ri-
vals, only Jaba loseliani has withdrawn his candidature,
and he was quick to do it in due time in the wake of a
series of incontrovertible “Mkhedrioni" disclosures. As
far as the purge is concerned, I admit it, if you please,

but let me put it right. The seeds sown will not grow if
the field has not been weeded. It is time for cleansing.
Georgia will emerge cleansed of what it has endured
through these five or six horrible years. However, a
catharsis is only possible on condition that all moral
ules are strictly observed. Therefore, it is not an elec-
toral alignment of forces, favorable solely for me, that I
am seeking, but a situation whereby blackmail, threats,
armed intimidation, and the use of huge sums in for-
eign currency for bribery, will be absolutely out of the
question. Georgia is parting from terrorism. Forever, I
hope. At least, this I wish to believe and this I wish to
work for as zealously as possible.

[Broladze} You took the initiative in appointing Gior-
gadze as head of the security service. Did you trust him?

[Shevardnadze] I had no reasons for distrust back then.
However, a year or a year and a half passed and I began
to have my doubts. After the assassination of Georgiy
Chanturiya, chairman of the National Democratic Party,
a person with an undoubtedly brilliant political future,
who had been elected leader of the Eastern Europe In-
ternational a day before the attack, some members of
the National Democratic Party’s board, and, above all,
Deputy Irina Sarishvili, Chanturiya’s widow, openly ac-
cused me of conniving with the assassins and demanded
that Igor Giorgadze be removed from his post for his
inability to expose the terrorist acts. How could anyone
know at that time that it was not a question of "non-
exposure,” but of staging an assassination, and later on,
a series of assassinatior3. It is noteworthy, though, that
the National Democrats also called for the dismissal of
Internal Affairs Minister Shota Kviraya. In actual fact,
however, it was under Kviraya’s leadership that the In-
ternal Affairs Ministry not only was firmly established,
but gave decisive battle to the mafia. The offensive con-
tinues.

Shevardnadze Issues Directive Abolishing Rescue
Corps

AU1210154795 Tbilisi SVOBODNAYA GRUZIYA
in Russian 5 Oct 95 pl

("Directive of the Georgian Head of State on the
Republic of Georgia Rescue Corps"}

{FBIS Translated Text] As one of the components of the
system of state administration, the Republic of Georgia
Rescue Corps was specially created in an extreme
situation to render help to the population and maintain
public order. It united, first and foremost, young people

CAUCASUS 83

who had taken an active part in fighting for Georgia’s
territorial integrity. Many of them deservedly received
State decorations for their bravery. The nation and
the country will never forget but will remember with
gratitude those members of the Mkhedrioni [Horsemen
— former paramilitary organization] who laid down
their lives in the struggle for the territorial integrity of
the motherland. Everything will be done to help those
who were maimed in battle and also while carrying
out their duties during natural disasters and in other
emergency situations.

At the same time, it is necessary to point out that
people with a dubious reputation joined the Rescue
Corps, people who not only changed the aims of this
association as laid down in its charter but also, as
competent bodies have established, finally transformed
the Rescue Corps into a haven for representatives of
the criminal world. Many members and heads of the
Rescue Corps, with the help of various clans and mafia
groups created with their assistance and patronage, have
committed illegal acts, involving the use of weapons,
violence, and blackmail. Many members of the Rescue
Corps have been convicted for committing various
serious crimes.

Some of the leaders of the Rescue Corps, who are
now under arrest, has been incriminated in the planning
and execution of terrorist acts. All this is hindering the
struggle for the establishment of strict public order in
the republic and is furthering the antistate and antisocial
activities of destructive forces.

Proceeding from this:

1. The Republic of Georgia Rescue Corps is to be
abolished.

2. In connection with the abolition of the Republic of
Georgia Rescue Corps, the Republic of Georgia Cabinet
of Ministers is to create a liquidation commission.

3. The liquidation commission is entrusted w. _ making
final payments to senior officials in the central apparatus
of the Rescue Corps and subunits subordinate to it in
accordance with the precedure provided for in Republic
of Georgia legislation.

4. The liquidation commission is to complete its work
within one month of its creation.

[Signed] E. Shevardnadze

Estonia

Meri Cancels Malta Trip Due To ‘Tapes Scandal’

WS1210115795 Tallinn BNS in English
1300 GMT 11 Oct 95

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Tallinn, Oct 11, BNS —
Estonian President Lennart Meri on Wednesday [11
October] said he was canceling his visit to Malta over
the so-called tapes scandal, which has led to the sacking
of the interior minister and to calls from leading parties
for resignation of the government.

Meri was scheduled to fly to Malta on Friday for
the annual Crans Montana Forum of politicians and
busiréss leaders.

The visit was canceled "because of serious problems in
the interior policy,” the presidential press office said.

Foreign Minister on ‘Constructive’ Meeting With
Kozyrev

LD1210204695 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1712 GMT 12 Oct 95

{FBIS Transcribed Text] TALLINN, Oct 12 (Interfax)
— Foreign Minister Riivo Sinijary has described as
“constructive” the meeting in Helsinki Wednesday with
his Russian counterpart Andrey Kozyrev.

Sinijarv told reporters in Tallinn Thursday that he had
informed Kozyrev about preparations in the Estonian
parliament to ratify agreements on pulling out Russian
troops and on social guarantees to Russian military
pensioners living in Estonia.

The documents signed in Moscow on July 26, 1994,
were later ratified by the Russian Duma and the Feder-
ation Council.

Sinijarv believes that “a certain degree of progress has
been achieved" in normalizing the Estonian-Russian
relations: the agreements on customs cooperation and
border representatives, and the protocol on cooperation
between the two countries’ foreign ministries have been
signed.

The two sides also reached understanding on another
neeting of teams of experts to draft an agreement on
the Russian-Estonian border, which is to be held on
October 23-24 on Russian territory. The venue has not
yet been set.

Sinijarv visited Finland where he discussed bilateral co-
Operations with Finnish Foreign Minister Tarja Halonen.
Kozyrev stayed in Finland on October 10-11 for an of-
ficial visit.

Defense Minister Comments on NATO Membership

LD1210210995 Tallinn Reklaamitelevisioon Network
in Estonian 1830 GMT 12 Oct 95

{[FBIS Translated Text] NATO Secretary General Willy
Claes stated that there is no longer any doubt about
Estonia joining NATO. Claes said that talks with NATO
may begin immediately.

Besides the subject of joining NATO, aid to Estonia
for setting up a control system for monitoring airspace
was discussed. According to Defense Minister Andrus
Oovel, NATO will support Estonia on the issue of
tuning the radars, on issues of funding, and training
staff.

[Begin Oovel recording] Proceeding from the fact that
one of the principles of the make-up of Estonian defense
forces is not the Estonian defense forces’ capability
to break and destroy but first and foremost to help
and save, our very first meeting was with General
Herbert van Foreest, the NATO's director assistant
secretary general for infrastructure, logistics and for
civil emergency planning. [end recording]

The present moment's preparedness was also under dis-
cussion. During a meeting with the Belgian Defense
Minister Jean-Paul Poncelet possibilities were discussed
for sending our officers for training to Belgium and the
admission of Estonian observers into the Belgian peace-
keeping mission. General George Joulwan, Supreme Al-
lied Commander Europe, emphasized in particular that
joining the Partnership for Peace program does not en-
sure full NATO membership. It is, however, an oppor-
tunity to develop a partner country’s cooperation ability.

[Begin Oovel recording] Whatever the security situa-
tion, we will always be able to approach the North At-
lantic Council as a partner country, as a country which
wants to proceed along the path leading to full NATO
membership. [end recording]

A new NATO peacekeeping mission will be undertaken
in the former Yugoslavia within the next month. In
November, U.S. Secretary of Defense William Perry and
NATO Secretary General Willy Claes will be arriving
in Estonia for familiarization.

Ministers Terms Russian NATO Pressure
‘Blackmail’

LD1210150395 Moscow INTERFAX in English
1040 GMT 12 Oct 95

[FBIS Transcribed Text] RIGA, Oct 12 (INTERFAX)
— Latvian Foreign Minister Valdis “srkavs believes
that Russia's attempts to exert pressure on the Baltic
states not to join NATO, verges on interference in
their internal affairs and can be likened to international
blackmail.

Such attempts are absolutely hopeless, he told Interfax
in comments on the information published by the
Komsomolskaya Pravda in late September to the effect
that Russia's General Staff is working out a new defense
doctrine in accordance with which Russian armed forces
will invade Estonia, Latvia and Lithuania, if they join
NATO.

Russia's behavior will even strengthen Latvia's resolve
to be integrated into NATO, Birkavs pointed out.

Unfortunately one has to admit that Russia does not
wish to understand NATO's essence as a union of
democratic nations and states following the road of
democracy, like Russia itself, Birkavs said.

Integration into NATO is a top priority in Latvia's
foreign policy concept adopted by the parliament and
government last March, he noted.

Ulmanis Postpones 3-6 Nov Visit to Krasnuvyarsk

LD1210151795 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English
1455 GMT 12 Oct 95

{BY Galina Kuchina]

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Riga, October 12 (ITAR-
TASS) — Latvian President Guntis Ulmanis postpones
a trips to the Krasnoyarsk ( crritory planned for Novem-
ber 3-6, ITAR-TASS learnt from the president's office
on Thursday. Guntis Ulmanis intended to go to the Kras-
noyarsk territory to meet Latvians residing in Siberia
and to open a consulate in Krasnoyarsk to facilitate the
procedure for Latvians getting visas for trips to Latvia.

BALTIC STATES 85

The president changed his plans because of the political
situation in the country resulting from the recent parlia-
mentary elections. The president also cancelled plans of
visiting and addessing a UNESCO conference in Paris.

However, Ulmanis’ planned visit to Sweden to begin
on October 16 is of special importance in the present
politicial situation, Ulmanis believes. He associates this
trip with forming Latvia's image. “If I gave up this visit
it would be regarded as a confirmation that things are
out of order in Latvia,” the president said.

Ulmanis is expected to speak on the forming of the
future cabinet on the local radio on Friday.
